**The re-seat.** ACE sends no F750 for a cast; it sends one on a wield or unwield (`Player_Inventory.cs` 306, 405; `Creature_Equipment.cs` 360, 436), on an enchantment's expiry (`EnchantmentManager.cs` 331) and on an item received. On a ready, buffed mage none of these is certain, so before Magic mode the leg moves the wielded wand to the pack (`MoveToPack`) and wields it again in its Held location (`Wield`), both through the Action API. That is the leg's fixture for D27's `sounds_server>=1`, not a gear step; the log says so (`buff: re-seating the wielded wand ...`). A wand in the pack is just wielded; no wand at all is left to the gear step. Whether the wield's F750 sounds (a wave exists) is first seen live: with `sounds_server=0` the leg fails with the D27 sentence.

**The verdict.** `[logincheck] buff done ok=<..> gear= worn= cast=ok|fizzled|failed|n/a buffs=<active>/<wanted> rebuffed= forced= min_remaining_s= levels= cycle_s= cast_scripts= cast_sounds= cast_windows= cast_frame_max_ms= sounds_server= fizzles= misses=` (`BuffLegVerdict.Compose`, offline-tested by `BuffLegVerdictTests`). `buffs=` prints the lines whose buff is active at the end (from the registry and, for the armour lines, the cast's per-piece chat lines) over the lines wanted, as the scenario does. Judged (`ok=`, D28): the scenario ended without a failure, `gear=wielded`, every wanted line active (`buffs=24/24`), `rebuffed=yes`, and `sounds_server>=1` (D27). Report-only in this gate: `cast=` (D20: `ok` when every cast that ended was confirmed or a fizzle its retry recovered, `fizzled` when a line was missed by fizzles (`fizzled`, `upgrade-fizzled`), `failed` for any other failure or miss, `n/a` when nothing was cast), `cast_scripts`, `cast_sounds`, `cast_windows` (deltas of `AvatarStats.CastScripts`, `CastSounds` and `CastWindows` around the scenario, `docs/avatars.md` section 17), `cast_frame_max_ms` (the worst frame while a cast window is open; the frames of the review captures are left out), `forced`, `min_remaining_s`, `levels`, `cycle_s`, `fizzles`, `misses`. `sounds_server` is the F750 voices started between the Enter and the scenario's end (`SoundLegTally`, one leg, `buff-leg`).

**The frames (review 2.1-R9, fix A).** With `--play-screenshot=PATH` and no `--play-screenshot-after`, the leg saves three 800x600 frames of the forced pass's cast (the last self buff of the cycle; a fully buffed mage casts only the forced line), with the developer HUD hidden: at the cast's effect time (the first script played on the player in the forced pass) plus 0.1, 0.4 and 0.8 s, as `<stem>-1.png`, `-2.png` and `-3.png`. Each path is logged with its delay (`buff: review frame N of 3 at the cast effect + D s: PATH`). The scenario is held up to 3 s while they are taken, so `cycle_s` includes the hold, about 1 s. A forced pass with no cast logs "the forced pass cast no self buff, so no frame was taken". The detail line's field is `review_frames` (it was `mid_cast_frame`). `PlayOptions.Parse` moves the path to `BuffShotPath` for this kind, so play mode's own capture (a fixed wait, then quit) does not fire; `PlayMode.PlayShotDeveloperHudNames` is `internal` for the leg. With `--play-screenshot-after` the old behaviour stays. The frames are derived from the retail dats' art and stay under `cache/`.

**Limits and run.** The runner's limit is `BuffLegRunner.WholeCheckSeconds`, 600 s, from the live measure (a cycle took 56 s of session time for 26 casts; the scripted worst case, 1,325 s, no longer sets it), and a log off after a failure may take 120 s more. The smoke step gives the Godot run 900 s (login, gear and a cycle of about 60 s). Run it live (the lead only, backend running, BSD Mage built): `pwsh -File tools\smoke.ps1 -Backend require -Only login-buff`, or `pwsh -File tools\godot.ps1 -Run -Offscreen -IsolateUserData -TimeoutSeconds 900 -Resolution 800x600 -GameArgs:'--account=test --login-check=buff --play-screenshot=cache/m3/2-1/buff-cast.png'`. The step is skipped under `-Backend auto` and `start` (it changes the character: buffs on, components burned) and slice-check never runs it. The `--login-check` help in `OptionCatalog.Play.cs` names the `buff` kind and the walking legs' judge; `--play-screenshot` names the three frames.

**Offline.** The verdict is tested without Godot (`BuffLegVerdictTests`); the smoke judge `Test-RecordedSources` is run for real by `BuffLegJudgesTests`; the runner's stage machine is not tested offline (no stand-in serves BSD Mage and a cast on the player's body is the game's own, `docs/avatars.md` section 17): the lead's live leg is its proof.

**Not done.** The `--buff-*` options are not read by the game leg (the scenario runs with the defaults): `TODO(m3-buff-leg-options)`.

**The verdict and the cast counts (M3 4.2 x3, x2).** The verdict line ends `surpassed= cast_scripts_expected=<casts of both passes>[ frame_over_50=yes]`. `cast_frame_max_ms` over 50 ms prints the flag and passes; over 75 ms it is not judged (`TODO(m3-buff-cast-frame-judge)`, M4). `cast_scripts=2` for a 26-cast cycle is explained: every recorded buff run has 3 to 6 cast windows (a hot character has little to recast), a self cast is one F755 on the player, and the 24 buff spells have no CasterEffect, only a TargetEffect; an armour cast is one F755 on an armour piece, which has no PhysicsScriptTable (the "type 88 ... table 0x00000000" warning), so it plays nothing, as retail's `play_script` does. `BuffCastScriptsTests` runs a cycle's casts through the avatar set (every self script played in its window; the armour ones counted `ServerScriptsMissing`).


## 31. The fight loop (M3 3.1; D10, D12, D21; DoD 3, 7, 8, 9, 11)

Item 3.1 puts BSD Mage in the Black Spawn Den and has it kill a Tusker Slave with Flame Bolt, by the Action API and `PlayerTools` only. The pieces: the den's data (`BlackSpawnDen`, the table queries), the counters (`FightTally`), the scenario (`FightScenario`, with its walk), the headless `--scenario=fight`, the game's leg `--login-check=bsd-fight` (`FightLegRunner`), the offline stand-in's fight (section 16) and the projectile on screen (`docs/avatars.md` section 17). The slice table is `docs/milestones/m3-plan.md` section 4.7.

**The den by generator, and the floor rule (D12; m3-plan section 4.7).** The den is landblock 0x0105. Three generators (wcid 3955) populate it: 0x70105022 in cell 0x0154 (Drudge Stalkers, Drudge Raveners, Tusker Guards), and 0x7010507A and 0x7010507B in cell 0x0382 (Virindi, Virindi Masters, Tusker Slaves, Tusker Guards). The arrival's floor (z 0, the cell 0x0375 of the Places row) holds only Tusker Slaves (wcid 1628): 17 spawns in 12 cells. The drudges and most of the virindi live in the north wing below it (z -24 to -30), with two virindi outliers among the tusker guards' cells. **The floor rule:** the loop's waypoints are that floor's cells, and its target filter takes wcid 1628 or 1629 whose cell is on that floor, never 23, 237, 1609 or 1610. The map is `cache/m3/3-1/den-map.md` (a read-only SELECT of `landblock_instance` and `landblock_instance_link`; `cache/` is gitignored).

**`BlackSpawnDen` and the Places row (`Session/Mage/BlackSpawnDen.cs`, `Interiors/InteriorLocations.cs`).** `BlackSpawnDen.Landblock` (0x0105), `FloorLowWords` (the floor's cell low words: the 13 cells of s0 and, since lane fa, the connected z 0 floor read from the dat's cell graph, 158 cells in all, minus the exit portal's room 0x0341-0x0343 and 0x035C, which `IsExcluded` holds), `Arrival` (a `CellFrame`), `IsExcluded(lowWord)`, `IsFloorCell(fullCell)` (a bit-mask lookup; exclusions win), `IsTarget(wcid, fullCell)` and `LeftFloor(cell)` (landblock 0x0105 and not a floor cell: the walks fail with "the walker left the den's floor" the moment it is true, review 3.1-R4; a settle off the floor fails with "settle: the player settled in cell ...", D12). The Places row is `InteriorLocations.BlackSpawnDenAluvian` (key `black-spawn-den-aluvian`, cell 0x01050375, (100, -210, 0), the portal's quaternion; in `All`, so `TeleportCatalog` and `PlayerTools.Place` reach it); `Dungeon(...)` takes an optional `source` string, and `CatalogWithInteriorsTests` pins 7 dungeons.

**`NearestCreature` and `HealthOf` (`Contracts/WorldObjectTable.cs`).** `NearestCreature(wcids, centre, maxMetres, cellFilter, out guid)` returns the nearest creature (`ItemType & Creature`, as `AvatarWire` reads it) of the listed weenie classes, by the 3D straight line in global metres (`CellFrame.ToGlobal`, so landblocks compare correctly). It skips a creature whose health is zero or below (an unknown health is kept), a creature whose forward motion is the Dead motion (`WorldObjectTable.DeadMotionLowWord`) and every guid in its `excluded` list (the scenario excludes a target whose kill notice named it, fix A). The filter is a cached delegate (`BlackSpawnDen.IsFloorCell` as a method group in a field), so the call allocates nothing. `HealthOf(guid, out fraction)` returns the last UpdateHealth fraction (false until one came). Retail's attackability and target choice (`ClientCombatSystem::ObjectIsAttackable` 11.6096 0x0056B340, `GetAttackTarget` 0x0056B630, `Handle_Combat__QueryHealthResponse` 0x0056B2A0) are cited, not read: the server stays the judge of attackability.

**`FightTally` (`Session/Scenarios/FightTally.cs`).** A bus subscriber (`Start(view)`, `Dispose`, `Reset`; `NoteCast`, `NoteMiss`) that counts, with no allocation per line (a hand parser over spans): `Casts`, `Misses`, `DamageDealt` and `DamageLinesDealt`, `CriticalsDealt`, `DrainDealt`, `DamageTaken` (the spell damage taken plus the 0x01B2 melee damage), `SpellDamageTaken`, `DrainTaken`, `HitsTaken` (0x01B2 only), `Evades` (0x01B4), `Kills` (0x01AD), `LastKillText`, `XpGained` (the 0x02CF total-experience delta from the baseline taken at `Start`) and `UnparsedMagicLines` (report-only, expected to be non-zero). **The damage-line forms** are F7E0 lines of chat type Magic (0x07) from ACE `SpellProjectile.cs` 810-845, each with the optional prefixes "Critical hit! ", "Overpower! ", "Sneak Attack! " in that order: "You <verb> <target> for <n> points with <spell>." (dealt), "With <spell> you drain <n> points of <vital> from <target>." (dealt, `DrainDealt`, not damage), "<source> <verbs> you for <n> points with <spell>." (taken) and "<source> casts <spell> and drains <n> points of your <vital>." (taken, `DrainTaken`). Any other Magic line (a resist, a cast notice, an expiry) is `UnparsedMagicLines`, never guessed. **The killing blow has no line:** ACE calls `OnDeath` instead of writing it (`SpellProjectile.cs` 857-861), so `damage_dealt` is the blows before the kill, and the kill is the 0x01AD notice. `TargetKills` and `LastKillNamedTarget` (review 3.1-R7, report-only) count a 0x01AD whose text contains the target's name from its CreateObject (no name: `TODO(m3-fight-target-name)`).

**`FightScenario` (`Session/Scenarios/FightScenario.cs`; `--scenario=fight`).** Stages (`FightStages` flags Gear, Buffs, Place, Mode; All for the headless run): start, gear (`GearScenario`'s steps), buffs, place (`PlayerTools.Place` to the den's row), teleporting, settle (3 s), mode-magic, fight, mode-peace, leave-den (the way out of the den before the end, `DenExit`; not in the arena-here run), logging-off, quitting, done. The buff top-up runs `BuffCycle` itself with a 600 s margin (`TopUpSeconds`; not `BuffScenario`, which would run the gear twice and cast the forced pass), and is skipped and logged when all 24 lines have 600 s or more. The fight's phases: choose, mana, cast, hit, dead-wait, walk.
- *The target* is `WorldObjectTable.NearestCreature(ArenaProfile.TargetWcids, player, range, BlackSpawnDen.IsFloorCell)` minus the dying and the named-dead.
- *The range* is ACE's (`Player_Magic.cs` 495-505): `min(BaseRangeConstant + (Init + Ranks) * BaseRangeMod, 75)` of the spell (`FightScenario.CastRange`, `MaxRange` 75 m), from the skill's start level plus ranks, NOT the buffed value (the plan said buffed; ACE wins, and an over-estimate costs only an out-of-range answer, 0x0550).
- *The Flame Bolt level (D10)* is the highest of Flame Bolt I-VII (ids 27, 81, 82, 83, 84, 85 and 2128) that the spellbook knows and `GearCasts.Chance` puts at or above `--buff-cast-chance` (0.9) from the buffed War Magic (`FightScenario.ChooseLevel`; the offline arena run takes it from a fixed skill, below). One fizzle drops one level, once. Flame Bolt is a regular line, so `TODO(m3-volley-blast-levels)` does not apply to the fight.
- *The miss rule.* A cast is a named miss unless it was Confirmed (the projectile's CreateObject, section 7's `Cast` row) and a damage line or the kill follows within 3 s (`HitWaitSeconds`). The marks for hit detection are taken when the cast is sent, because the damage line can arrive in the frame of the confirmation. Three misses in a row (`MissesBeforeWalking`), `MissileOutOfRange` (0x0550) or no target in range walk one leg. A dead target waits up to 2 s (`KillNoticeSeconds`) for the 0x01AD. Mana short waits (no transfer or revitalize in the loop) and counts against `--fight-seconds`.
- *The limits.* The fight ends when `kills >= --fight-kills` AND `damage_dealt > 0` (fix A, DoD 8): a first hit that kills leaves `damage_dealt` 0, so the loop logs "fighting on until a damage line" once and goes on, bounded by `--fight-seconds`; the leg's `kills` can then exceed `--fight-kills`. The timeout reads `kills=K of N and damage_dealt=D`. The player's death, a walk that fails or stops without arriving, a SpellTable without the level, a read model without War Magic and a leg with no walker (`TODO(m3-fight-no-walker)`) fail loudly.
- *The walk.* The stock `CellRoutePlanner.IsWalkable` refuses the den's plaza doorways, whose planes lean 11 degrees (normal Z 0.196), so it reaches 1 spawn cell of 12 (`TODO(m3-fight-planner-lean)`). `DenPlanner` is the replacement: a BFS over the floor cells; the tour order is `DenWalk.Tour`, nearest the arrival first. `DenWalk` (headless) is an `OfflineWalker` in landblock 0x0105's collision with the `RoutePlay` wiring minus the audit (`TODO(m3-fight-walk-audit)`: the legs are not checked against ACE's positions). The game's walker is `GameFightWalk` (below). `IFightWalk` is the seam (`Prepare`, `BeginLeg`, `Tick`, `Abandon`, `Failure`).
- *Verdict fields* (`FightScenario.VerdictFields()`, printed by the headless host as `fight <fields>` before `headless done`): the leg's fields below, and `target_kills=` last.

**Headless `--scenario=fight`.** `HeadlessScenario.Fight` (BSD Mage on the test account only, as gear and buff: `--character` and `--create-character` are ignored with a warning and any other `--account` is refused). The run builds the den's collision and floor graph for `DenWalk`, then runs the scenario with `FightStages.All`. Options (`OptionCatalog.Fight.cs`, group "Fight loop", read by the main scene and the headless host): `--fight-kills` (1 to 1000, default 1) and `--fight-seconds` (default 300, session-clock seconds from the first cast stage); a bad value fails the run at once. The default time limit is 3,600 s (`Program.FightQuitAfterSeconds`: the gear, a top-up of up to 1,300 s, the fight, the log off). The `--buff-*` options of section 29 reach the headless run's top-up, not the game leg's (`TODO(m3-buff-leg-options)`). A failure at any stage logs off first.

**`FightLiveTests` (`tests/Lifestone.Loopback.Tests/Session/FightLiveTests.cs`, `[LoopbackFact]`; the lead only).** Runs `FightScenario` against the real ACE (the mod ready, BSD Mage built): the scenario ends without a failure at stage "done"; `kills >= 1` and `damage_dealt > 0`; projectiles seen and logged with their create position, velocity and Setup; not every cast a miss; the player did not die; the level is I-VII. It is what proved the projectile attribution rule live (below).

**The leg: `--login-check=bsd-fight`.** A kind of its own, in the style of section 30: never part of `--login-check=all`, hooked by one line (`FightLegRunner.Install`) in `LoginCheckRunner.Install`. The game enters BSD Mage on the test account (absent: it fails loudly, "run --scenario=mage-build"), waits for the arrival (Travel Present, 3 s settle) and runs `FightScenario` in the game's session with `FightStages.All` (the gear, the buff top-up, the place, Magic mode, Flame Bolt at the tuskers of the arrival's floor until `--fight-kills` kills or `--fight-seconds`, non-combat), then logs off and quits. A failure at any stage logs off before it is reported. A bad `--fight-kills` or `--fight-seconds` fails the leg at its first frame ("bad fight option: ...", review 3.1-R11); the options help says the game leg's buff top-up ignores the `--buff-*` options.
- *The game's player is the walker.* `GameFightWalk` (`scripts/Session/`) is the game's `IFightWalk`: it plans each leg with `DenPlanner.Plan` over the den's floor graph (built on the loading worker) from the player's walker position and drives `walker.Controller` with a `RouteDriver`, as `RoutePlay`'s hosted mode does through `RoutePlayHost` (the leg gives play mode one at install, before the player is built). Movement goes out through the host's sink (the presence gate), unaudited. `BeginLeg` and `Tick` fail loudly when the player's cell is in landblock 0x0105 and not a floor cell (review 3.1-R4). `TODO(m3-fight-no-walker)` is closed for the game.
- *The loading worker* reads the SpellTable, builds the `GearKit` (the login name is hashed inside the worker and stays in its locals), loads the den's floor graph (`LandblockCellSet.Load`, `DenPlanner.FloorOf`) and calls `RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod` over every method of the `Lifestone.Client.Session.Scenarios` and `.Mage` namespaces and of `GameFightWalk`, `FightLegRunner` and `RouteDriver` (the log line "the fight's methods were prepared on the loading worker" carries the count and the milliseconds), so none of the fight's code is compiled on the game thread in the den.
- *The verdict.* `[logincheck] bsd-fight done ok= kills= damage_dealt= spells_cast= misses= xp_gained= damage_taken= hits_taken= evades= projectiles= cast_frame_max_ms= fight_frame_max_ms= bsd_min_fps= fight_s= bolt_light= dyn_lights_max= viewer_light=` (`FightLegVerdict.Compose`, `FightLegVerdictTests`). The three light fields (M3 4.1 lane c; omitted when no frame was sampled): `bolt_light=yes|no` (every projectile that flew at least 3 frames had its own entry in `DynamicLightFrame` for at least 90% of its frames, `LightFrameSampler`; judged in the offline fight loop, report-only live), `dyn_lights_max=<n>`, `viewer_light=on|off`. **Judged:** no failure, `kills >= 1`, `damage_dealt > 0`. **Report-only** (a new judge is report-only in its first gate; 3.2 judges the worst frame against 50 ms, worst of three, and `damage_taken` and `hits_taken`, DoD 9): everything else. `damage_dealt` excludes the killing blow. `projectiles` is the delta of `AvatarStats.ProjectilesShown` over the leg (a Flame Bolt's one GfxObj degrades to nothing: "shown" is what is counted, and the bolt on screen is its particle trail; review 3.1-R6 renamed the counter from `ProjectilesDrawn`, the field keeps its name). A detail line follows: `bsd-fight detail seconds= level= range_m= walks= projectiles_created= projectile_explodes= projectile_windows= cast_windows= frames= den_frames= fps_windows= worst_fight_frame_at_s= worst_fight_frame_ms= worst_session_tick_ms= worst_pump_messages= worst_pump_ms= worst_retail_ui_step_ms= worst_play_mode_step_ms= worst_play_mode_work_ms= worst_play_mode_adapter_ms= worst_play_mode_events_applied= review_frames= scenario_verdict=` (the frame attribution of the slowest fight frame).
- *The frame measures* (`FightFrameSampler`, Client; one call per frame, no allocation; the engine's delta is the previous frame's whole time). `cast_frame_max_ms`: the worst frame while the cast window is open (the buff top-up's casts included). `fight_frame_max_ms`: the worst frame of the fight stage while a cast window or a projectile window (`AvatarSet.ProjectileWindowOpen`) is open or the loop has a target engaged (`FightScenario.Target != 0`). `bsd_min_fps`: the lowest one-second window of frames in the den (the player present, landblock 0x0105; the window in progress at the end, a window a skipped frame fell in and a window the player left are not counted). The frame after a saved review frame is left out of all three (the read-back stalls it).
- *The review frames.* With `--play-screenshot=PATH` and no `--play-screenshot-after` (`PlayOptions.Parse` moves the path to `BuffShotPath` for `bsd-fight` as for `buff`), the leg saves four 800x600 frames with the developer HUD hidden from the fight stage's start. `<stem>-bolt.png`: the first projectile created after the first damage line (the target in sight), plus 0.15 s. `<stem>-hit.png`: the Explode of a projectile whose damage line comes within 1 s of it (either order, paired by time), plus 0.1 s from that Explode; an Explode on a wall with no damage line gives no hit frame (review 3.1-R3). `<stem>-kill.png`: the 0x01AD kill plus 0.5 s, the kill line in the chat. `<stem>-viewer.png` (M3 4.1 lane c): the player in the den with no projectile alive for 1 s (logged as "no projectile alive for 1 s in the den"), the viewer light round it; it can fire before the first cast and holds the scenario a few frames. Flights under 3 frames are counted, not judged (`TODO(m3-bolt-light-short-flight)`). `FightShotPlan.Begin/Step(created, explodes, damageLines, kills)` is the pure plan: at most one frame a frame, two hidden frames before a save, each slot triggered once; the scenario is held while a frame is owed, at most 3 s after its trigger (past it the frame is given up and named). The log carries `fight: review frame <name> at its trigger + D s: PATH` for each and `fight: review frames: bolt=taken hit=taken kill=taken` at the end (a slot whose trigger never came reads `trigger never came`; it is never faked). The frames are derived from the retail dats and stay under `cache/`.
- *Limits.* `FightLegRunner.WholeCheckSecondsFor(fightSeconds)` = login 60 + gear 120 + buff top-up 240 + teleport and settle 30 + `--fight-seconds` (default 300) = 750 s; a log off after a failure may take 120 s more (`RecoverySeconds`); the smoke step gives the Godot run 1200 s (a test pins 1200 >= 750 + 120 + 300).
- *Run it live* (the lead only, backend running, BSD Mage built): `pwsh -File tools\smoke.ps1 -Backend require -Only login-bsd`, or with the frames `pwsh -File tools\godot.ps1 -Run -Offscreen -IsolateUserData -TimeoutSeconds 1200 -Resolution 800x600 -GameArgs:'--account=test --login-check=bsd-fight --play-screenshot=cache/m3/3-1/fight.png'`.

**The smoke step `login-bsd`** (`tools/smoke.ps1`): `--account=test --login-check=bsd-fight`, 1200 s, a backend step run ONLY under `-Backend require` (SKIP under `auto` and `start`: it changes the character, with experience gained and components burned). Its judge is `bsd-fight done ok=true` and exit code 0, and it prints the verdict line. It sits in `$backendSteps` after `login-buff`; slice-check never runs it.

**The attribution rule, live (`TODO(m3-cast-projectile-attribution)`, D21).** Live (`FightLiveTests` and the `bsd-fight` leg, 2026-10-17): every one of the player's Flame Bolts was created at the player (about 1.2 m up, within the 5 m rule) with Flame Bolt's Setup 0x0200040D and a velocity of about 15 m/s along the aim, and each was taken; no other Missile object came near the player in the den, so the rule held. The wire still names no caster, so the TODO stays open for a crowd of casters (section 7, `Cast`).

**The whole loop offline** (`PlayChecks.Fight.cs`, after fb's own check, section 16). `FightScenario` with `FightOptions { ArenaHere = true }` runs against a second stand-in tusker (`StandInTusker.Make`, guid 0x80000B02, placed ahead of the player inside the range of level I). "Arena here" masks the stages to Mode (no gear, no top-up, no teleport), turns the cell filter off (any cell is a target) and takes the Flame Bolt level and the range from `FightOptions.ArenaSkill` (default 300, the stand-in's fixed skill, `TODO(m3-standin-magic-skill)`) through `FightScenario.ChooseLevel(uint skill, ...)`, with no spellbook: the stand-in's spell learn sends no magic update, so its book never holds Flame Bolt II to VII (`TODO(m3-standin-spellbook-updates)`; a first run chose level I, 1 point a blow). It is not on the command line (an init property). Three plain `Check`s and one `fight loop done` log line: the setup; the leg's own judge (`FightLegVerdict.Compose`: `kills >= 1`, `damage_dealt > 0`); and `projectiles_shown >= 1 projectile_explodes >= 1` over the fight (D28). The sampler's cast and fight maxima are reported.

**The offline play-checks of the item.** fb's check (`PlayChecks.Fight.cs`) is six plain `Check`s on the stand-in's tusker (casts, confirmed, kills, `damage_dealt`, damage lines, `xp_gained`, and the per-cast lines, hits, misses and kills) and a `fight done` log line. fc's check (`PlayChecks.Projectile.cs`), `a-stand-in-flame-bolt-is-shown-and-flies`, judges `projectiles_created>=1`, `projectiles_shown>=1`, that it moved and that its window opened and closed; it reports `wait_frames`, launches, explodes and pipelines, and a report-only line `projectile with meshes` (Shock Wave I). A shown Flame Bolt is not checked for its particles (the renderer has no per-object count, review 3.1-R6). The play-checks ran 172 checks at fd's last count (max frame 33.5 ms, offscreen); `a-stand-in-flame-bolt-...` and the Shock Wave line are in `docs/avatars.md` section 17.

**Option `--retail-sphereless-missile`** (`OptionCatalog.Play.cs`, `PlayOptions.RetailSphereless`, passed to `AvatarSet.RetailSpherelessMissile` where `PlayMode` sets `RetailUnhideFlash`): see `docs/avatars.md` section 17. It closes `TODO(m3-sphereless-missile)`.

**Tests.** `BlackSpawnDenTests`, `WorldObjectTableQueryTests`, `FightTallyTests` (s0); `FightScenarioTests` (scripted runs, the dat-backed planner and level tests, the arena-here run, `ChooseLevel(skill)`); `HeadlessFightOptionsTests`; `StandInFightTests` (fb); `ProjectileFlightTests` (fc, `docs/avatars.md` section 16); `FightLegRunnerTests` (sampler, plan, verdict wiring, option routing, the smoke step and its time) and `FightLegVerdictTests`; `GameChatTests` gained two (`docs/retail-ui.md` section 21.2). The runner's stage machine is not tested offline (no stand-in serves BSD Mage and the player body is the game's own), and `GameFightWalk` has no offline test (the Game project has none): the lead's live leg is its proof.

**Open TODO names of 3.1.** `m3-fight-walk-audit` (the legs are not audited against ACE), `m3-fight-planner-lean`, `m3-fight-left-floor` (named in the "left the floor" failure, for a planner or physics gap), `m3-fight-target-name`, `m3-standin-magic-skill`, `m3-standin-spellbook-updates`, `m3-cast-projectile-attribution` (above), the stand-in's own (section 16) and the projectile's (`m3-bolt-cell-tracking`, `m3-bolt-warm-up`; `docs/avatars.md` section 17). Closed: `m3-fight-leg`, `m3-fight-no-walker`, `m3-sphereless-missile` (the flag), `m3-standin-projectile-model`, `m3-standin-cast-plan-lazy`. Not applying: `m3-volley-blast-levels`. All in `docs/milestones/m3-plan.md` section 9.

**The clear-line pick and the wall shots (M3 4.2 p4, fix A).** The bolt goes at the nearest tusker with a clear line (`IFightWalk.HasClearLine`; `GameFightWalk`: `PhysicsWorld.SweepViewer`, a 0.3 m sphere, both ends at 2/3 of a 1.8 m body). None clear walks at once. A bolt that explodes short (velocity zero 0.6 s with no damage line, stopped before 80% of its flight) is a wall hit, and the walk moves on to the first clear one; `wall_hits=` is report-only beside `misses=`, and `bolt_light=no` and `viewer_light=off` fail every bsd-fight leg (`bolt_light` reads n/a when no flight had three frames). A wall hit is judged by distance: the bolt's stop point more than `FightScenario.WallStopShortMetres` (4 m) short of the target (`TODO(m3-fight-target-radius)`); a resist line since the cast is never a wall hit. `--fight-ignore-line` restores the old pick. The cause: the arrival cell 0x0375 and the tuskers' 0x0383 are 44.2 m apart through rock, and ACE and the client check no line (`ClientMagicSystem::CastSpell` 0x00568DE0). The sweeps cost 0.03 to 0.15 ms each for the den's longest blocked picks (fix A), so no cache is needed. Named: `TODO(m3-fight-los-radius)`, `m3-fight-los-target-height`, `m3-fight-los-approximation`, `m3-fight-wall-impact-point` (not built: the impact point is not logged), `m3-fight-approach-target` (not built), `m3-standin-wall-clip` (not built: the stand-in still flies bolts through walls), `m3-den-room-statics`. Tests: `FightLineOfSightTests` (the arrival is blocked to 0x0383; the 13-cell table pinned), five scenario tests, three verdict tests and one frame-plan test. The review frames are `-first`, `-bolt`, `-hit`, `-kill` and `-viewer`; `-first` is 0.15 s after the first projectile is created.


## 32. The death loop (M3 3.2; D17, D33-D36; DoD 9, 10, 11)

Item 3.2 has BSD Mage bound at Holtburg's lifestone, unbuffed in the Black Spawn Den, hit and killed by the Tusker Slaves, respawned at the lifestone with vitae and 75% vitals, and re-geared, by the Action API and `PlayerTools` only. The pieces: the two new actions (section 7), `NearestWithFlags`, the counters (`DeathTally`), the verdict (`DeathLegVerdict`), the scenario (`DeathScenario` with its approach walk), the headless `--scenario=death-and-regear`, the loopback tests, the game's leg `--login-check=bsd-death` (`DeathLegRunner`, with `DeathFrameSampler` and `DeathShotPlan`), the offline stand-in's death (section 16) and the three combat windows (`docs/retail-ui.md` section 23.8). The slice table is `docs/milestones/m3-plan.md` section 4.8.

**What ACE does at a player's death** (`Player_Death.cs` 34-292; the plan's section 4.8 preamble has the line numbers). In this order: `OnDeath` sends the VictimNotification 0x01AC to the player (and 0x019E to the others); `Die` sends the Dead motion (F74C, stance NonCombat), 0x02E9 health 0, 0x02CD NumDeaths, DeathLevel and VitaeCpPool, the vitae 0x02C2 (spell 666, 5% a death, at most 40%: `EnchantmentManager.cs` 391-443) and then **0x02C6 with every enchantment removed** (211-216; BSD Mage has no `AugmentationSpellsRemainPastDeath`). After the Dead animation plus 1 s: the lost items, then the corpse (`CreateCorpse`, `Creature_Death.cs` 428: a level-126 player drops level/10 plus 0 to 2 items by value, wielded ones included, 347-540; a wielded item leaves by `TryDequipObjectWithNetworking(ToCorpseOnDeath)` and the client sees 02DA Wielder 0, 02CE 0, F74A, the unwield sound and F747, with **no** 0x0024), and in the same tick the teleport through portal space (F751) to `Sanctuary ?? Instantiation`, then NonCombat, the lifestone protection (60 s, `SetLifestoneProtection`) and, 3 s after the arrival, 0x02E9 for health, stamina and mana at 75% of the maximum (262-282). So the corpse's CreateObject and the F751 come in one tick. BSD Mage's sanctuary is its creation spot until it uses a lifestone (`Lifestone.cs` 44-75: the Sanctuary motion, the attune line "You have attuned your spirit to this Lifestone. You will resurrect here after you die.", half the stamina). ACE answers a Use of the lifestone with UseDone before that chain ends, and moves an out-of-range player (`CreateMoveToChain`) only while the client follows its MoveToObject. **The protection is dispelled by any teleport** (`Player_Location.cs` 702-703, `Player_Death.cs` 937-942: "Your actions have dispelled the Lifestone's magic!"; it also ends by itself after 60 s with "You're no longer protected by the Lifestone's magic!", 934), so a loop that reaches the den by a teleport never meets the protected blow (0x028A 0x0502, `Player_Combat.cs` 299, 416-418, 480-482; M3 3.2 review R2): `protected=` stays 0 live and the protected blow is proved offline only (below).

**The suicide (D33).** ACE has a player-side suicide: GameAction 0x0279 (`GameActionDie`; `Player_Death.cs` 299-345): five HearSpeech lines 3 s apart (15 s), then `Die` without 0x01AC and 0x019E and with WeenieError YouKilledYourself after the NumDeaths update; WeenieError YoureTooBusy while the player is dead or teleporting; a second request while one is in progress is dropped without a word; `HandleSuicide` stops when NumDeaths changed meanwhile (the tuskers killed the player first). `IActionApi.Suicide` and `AddSpellFavorite` are in section 7. A death removes every enchantment, so the loop takes one suicide in the den first when any enchantment but the vitae is active: the purge makes the mage unbuffed, the corpse stays in the den (never in Holtburg, where the baseline's laps run), and a buffed run dies twice (two vitae steps).

**`NearestWithFlags` (`Contracts/WorldObjectTable.cs`).** `NearestWithFlags(descriptionFlags, in centre, maxMetres, out guid)` returns the nearest object with a position whose CreateObject description flags contain any of the bits, in `NearestCreature`'s frame (global metres, no allocation): `WorldObjectTable.LifestoneFlag` 0x4000 (`BF_LIFESTONE`) and `CorpseFlag` 0x2000 (`BF_CORPSE`), as `WorldContentCensus` reads them.

**`DeathTally` (`Session/Scenarios/DeathTally.cs`).** A bus subscriber like `FightTally` (`Start(view)`, `Dispose`, `Reset`; no allocation per message) that reads ACE's order: `VictimNotices` (PlayerDied from 0x01AC only), `DeadMotions` (the player's F74C with forward Dead), `Deaths` (the NumDeaths delta, a suicide included), `VitaePercent` ((1 - the multiplier of spell 666) x 100, rounded), `Purges` (EnchantmentChanged Purged or PurgedBad: only a purge that removed something, because the reducer publishes nothing for an empty one, `TODO(m3-purge-empty)`; so the tuskers' death of an unbuffed mage reads `purged=0` while ACE did send 0x02C6, and DoD 10's "0x02C6 seen" is read at the suicide's purge of a buffed mage), `DeathTeleports` (the first F751 after a death signal), `HasArrival`, `ArrivalCell` and `ArrivalFrame` (the self-teleport position after it), `ProtectedHits` (WeenieError 0x0502 `LifestoneMagicProtectsYou`), the shares of the vitals (`HealthAfterArrivalPercent` and the stamina and mana ones, the **first** 0x02E9 of each vital after the arrival: ACE's three at +3 s, so the regeneration of the next minute does not move them, review R8; `VitalsAfterArrivalPercent` the lowest of the three once all three landed, NaN before; the share is the current over the maximum as the state computes it, so the vitae's reduction of the maximum is inside the denominator, 21/27 at 5%), `HealthMinPercent` and the embedded `Fight` (`HitsTaken`, `DamageTaken`, `Evades`). The killing blow has no 0x01B2, so a blow that kills is not a hit taken.
- *The corpse window (review R1).* `CorpsesSeen` and `LastCorpseGuid` count a CreateObject with the corpse flag whose name is exactly "Corpse of " + the player's name (ordinal) **only between a death signal and that death's arrival** (`_afterDeath || _awaitingArrival`); `DeathFrameSampler` uses the same rule (`_awaitingPortal || _awaitingArrival`). ACE keeps the earlier runs' corpses in the den and re-creates them for the client at every arrival and whenever one comes into view, so the live `corpse=7` and `corpse=9` of the first rounds counted old ones; the others are `StaleCorpses`, printed as `corpses_stale=` (report-only).
- *The protection line (review R2).* `ProtectionDispels` and `ProtectionExpiries` count the two Magic chat lines above; `Protection` is `dispelled`, `expired` or `none` (the last line wins) and prints as `protection=` (report-only). A live buffed run shows `dispelled` (the den teleport after the respawn); an unbuffed one with no teleport after its respawn shows `none` or `expired`.

**`DeathLegVerdict` (`Session/Scenarios/DeathLegVerdict.cs`).** `[logincheck] bsd-death done ok= died= suicides= vitae= respawn=lifestone|elsewhere|none corpse= purged= protected= vitals_pct= gear_restored=yes|no worn= hits_taken= damage_taken= evades= health_min_pct= death_s= death_frame_max_ms= fight_frame_max_ms= deaths= corpses_stale= protection=dispelled|expired|none corpse_looted=yes|partial|no|skipped items_recovered=<n> trip_s=<x|n/a> trip_abort=<reason|none> rebuffed=yes|no|skipped den_kills=<n> decoys=<n|n/a> decoy_safe=yes|no|n/a decoys_dropped=<n|n/a> gear_dropped=<n|n/a> decoys_recovered=<n|n/a> pretrip_heals=<n|n/a> gear_left=<n|n/a> mode_mismatch=<n>[ frame_over_50=yes]` (no name, no account; the fields are additive, a new one goes last). **Judged:** no failure, `died == 1` (exactly one death by the tuskers: the victim notices; the suicide sends none), `respawn == lifestone` (`ClassifyRespawn`: the arrival within `LifestoneRadiusMetres` = 15 m of a `BF_LIFESTONE` object in the table), `gear_restored == yes`, `damage_taken > 0`, `hits_taken >= 1` (DoD 9, D36: judged in the unbuffed leg; report-only in `bsd-fight`). **Judged** (M3 4.1 lane c): `death_frame_max_ms` over 75 ms fails; over 50 ms the line ends with `frame_over_50=yes`. **Report-only:** everything else, `vitae` (D35) included. The M4 2.2 s1 fields `corpse_looted=`, `items_recovered=`, `trip_s=` and `trip_abort=` are report-only too: `corpse_looted=yes` = every entry of the opened corpse came (no refusal, timeout or unresolved skip), the corpse was closed and nothing aborted; `partial` = some but not all came (an abort, such as the 40% health guard among the tuskers, included: the pick-up in flight at an abort is drained and counted); `no` = a trip began and none came; `skipped` = no trip (the option or the stage is off, or a precondition failed).  The verdict's `vitals_pct` is each vital's value read at the END of the respawn's 5 s wait (after ACE's 3 s restore, `Player_Death.cs` 262-282; the first 0x02E9 after the arrival can be an earlier regeneration, 2.2 review R1), never the trip's. `DeathScenario.VerdictFields()` carries them before ` bars= unbuff= bound=`. `DeathLegVerdict.Measure(tally, objects, suicides, gearRestored, worn, deathSeconds, deathFrameMaxMs, fightFrameMaxMs)` reads a tally and the object table. `FightLegVerdict` judges the frames since 3.2 s0: `cast_frame_max_ms` or `fight_frame_max_ms` over 75 ms fails, over 50 ms the line ends ` frame_over_50=yes` and the run passes (the lead reads the worst of three); an unmeasured frame is not judged. `damage_taken` and `hits_taken` stay report-only there.

**The death leg's new fields (M4 3.1 d1, d2; report-only in their first gate).** `rebuffed=` is yes when the inner scenario's buff stage ended with every profile line up (its `buffs=` note `skipped`, or `topped-up:<casts>` with `BuffCycle.Result.Active` equal to `Wanted`: `IFightLeg.BuffsAllUp`), no when it failed or ended with a line down, and skipped when the stage is off or never ran; `den_kills=` the inner fight's kills (0 when it did not run); `decoys=` the decoys the pack holds when the wait among the tuskers begins (`DecoyKit.IsDecoy` by weenie class; the unbuff suicide is a death too and can have dropped some, so the line is logged with a second `DecoyKit.Check` and a warning when it is not safe); `decoy_safe=` the gear stage's `DecoyCheck.Field` (`n/a` with decoys off or with no gear stage); `decoys_dropped=`, `gear_dropped=` and `decoys_recovered=` the listing's counts (above); `pretrip_heals=` the casts of the pre-trip heal. `n/a` stands for "not measured", never 0. The line carries `mode_mismatch=<n>` before the optional `frame_over_50=yes` (M4 3.1 g's `CombatModeWatch`, started with the scenario and stopped at its end or its failure; report-only; its first violation is logged as a warning and the detail line carries `mode_mismatch_unchecked=`).

**`DeathScenario` (`Session/Scenarios/DeathScenario.cs`, `DeathOptions.cs`; `--scenario=death-and-regear`).** `DeathStages` flags Gear, Bind, Den, Unbuff, Death, Regear, Vitae, Corpse, Rebuff = 256 and Fight = 512 (All). Each stage is logged once as `[death] stage <name>`: gear, bars, bind-place, bind-teleporting, bind-settle, bind-use, bind-line, den-place, den-teleporting, den-settle, unbuff, unbuff-dying, unbuff-arrival, unbuff-vitals, death-mode, death-wait, respawn, respawn-vitals, corpse-teleporting, corpse-settle, corpse-seek, corpse-opening, corpse-looting, corpse-closing, corpse-return, corpse-return-settle, regear, regear-settle, logging-off, quitting, done. M4 3.1 d1 and d2 add `unbuff-decoys` (after `unbuff-vitals`, only after a suicide with decoys on) and `rebuff`, `rebuff-peace`, `fight` and `pretrip-heal` (only when the health share is the one failing trip precondition). The order after `respawn-vitals` (`DeathScenario.Order.cs`): with the fight on (the default) `regear` (the plain kit, `kit.WithDecoys([])`, so the decoys on the corpse are not spawned a second time), `regear-settle`, `rebuff`, `rebuff-peace` (when the rebuff runs alone), `fight`, `pretrip-heal`, `corpse-teleporting` ... `corpse-return-settle`, `done`; with the fight off, M4 2.2's order (`corpse-*`, `regear`, then `rebuff` alone when it is on); both off is the 2.2 loop. `DeathLegRunner` runs `DeathStages.All`, so the order is chosen by `--death-rebuff on|off` and `--death-fight on|off` (default on; bad values are errors). Rebuff and fight are ONE composed inner `FightScenario` (an `IFightLeg`, from the `FightLegFactory` seam; the default `DeathScenario.DefaultFightLeg` wraps the real scenario), stepped as the regear's `GearScenario` is: `FightStages.Buffs` for the rebuff (the death purged every enchantment, so it is the full cycle), `Place | Mode` and the kill loop for the fight (`--death-fight-kills`, default 4; `--death-fight-seconds`, default 180); the rebuff alone drops the inner scenario at its own stage `place` and the mage leaves the magic mode (`rebuff-peace`). `DeathOptions.Rebuff`, `Fight`, `FightKills` and `FightSeconds`; `--death-stages` also takes `rebuff` and `fight`.
- *Gear and the spell bars.* The gear stage is `GearScenario`'s steps. The bars are inside the gear flag: `DeathScenario.PlanBars(Spellbook)` puts on bar `ProfileBar` the highest of its first seven spells the spellbook knows for each of the 31 arena profile lines (a line with none is left off and warned), and on bar `BoltBar` Flame Bolt I-VII (ids 27, 81-85 and 2128), each appended after what the bar already holds and only when absent, so a second login plans nothing (`bars=set:<n>` or `bars=ok`). **The bars are 0-based, as ACE's bar id (0 to 7): `ProfileBar` = 1 and `BoltBar` = 2 are the second and third tabs (II and III) of the spellcasting window (review R7); the first tab keeps whatever the character had.** The plan's "bar 1" and the highest-of-I-VII choice are the lane's reading; the owner changes either with one constant. ACE answers `AddSpellFavorite` with nothing and the read model's `SpellBars` changes only with the next PlayerDescription, so each is sent once and no echo is waited for.
- *The bind (D34).* `PlayerTools.Place` to Holtburg's point of interest (`TeleportCatalog.FromAce` of `AcePointsOfInterest` "Holtburg", sent as `place poi`), a 3 s settle, `NearestWithFlags(LifestoneFlag, player, 60 m)` (remembered: `LifestoneGuid`, `LifestonePosition`), then **the approach walk**, then `IActionApi.Use`. The walk is `IApproachWalk` (`Session/Scenarios/DeathApproach.cs`: `Prepare`, `Begin(target, stopWithin)`, `Tick`, `Abandon`), passed as the scenario's last constructor parameter `approach:`: the stop radius is the lifestone CreateObject's `UseRadius` (centre to centre, at least 0.8 m), else the named placeholder 1.5 m (`LifestoneApproachMetres`, `TODO(m3-lifestone-radius)`); nothing walks when the player is already within it; the walk is bounded by `ApproachSeconds` 30; a walk that ends stuck, failed or late still sends the Use (ACE's range check judges, loudly); with no walker the Use goes out with a warning (`TODO(m3-bind-approach)`). Headless it is `HoltburgApproach.Create(dats, log)` (an `OfflineWalker` over Holtburg's landblock and its eight neighbours with a `RouteDriver` over a one-goal route; it places a still-unplaced body from `Self.Position` as a fallback), in the game `GameApproachWalk` (below). The scenario ticks a prepared approach walker every frame from the Holtburg place until the bind takes, as `DenWalk` is ticked. **The attune line:** ACE answers UseDone before its chain sets the sanctuary, so the bind is done on the lifestone's Magic chat line (any Magic line after the Use within `BindLineSeconds` 15) plus the UseDone, logged as `bind: the lifestone's chat line: ...` (the text is the world database's, never guessed); a WeenieError of `BindFailureCodes` (0x001D YoureTooBusy, 0x0036 ActionCancelled, 0x0037 ObjectGone, 0x0498 YouHaveMovedTooFar, 0x049C YouWanderedTooFarToLinkWithLifestone, 0x058D YouCannotUseThatItem) or the timeout fails "the bind did not take" with the distance; the other WeenieErrors (0x051C chat channel joins) are logged as ignored. Distances are logged before the walk, after it and at the Use.
- *The den and the unbuff (D33).* `PlayerTools.Place` to the Black Spawn Den's row (section 31), a 3 s settle. When `ActiveEnchantments` holds any spell that a death's purge removes (not 666, not an item's lasting spell with duration -1, not a cooldown with a spell id above 0x7FFF: what ACE's `RemoveAllEnchantments` keeps, `EnchantmentManager.cs` 358-365, and the reducer keeps on 0x02C6) the scenario logs "unbuff: the mage is buffed (<n> enchantments); a suicide purges them as every death does", sends `Suicide()`, waits for the death, the death's teleport and the arrival (+5 s for ACE's 3 s of vitals), checks that none of those remains (else a loud failure) and runs the den stages again; `unbuff=suicide|none|killed|n/a`. **The race:** the tuskers hit the buffed mage during the suicide's 15 s countdown; if they kill it first (a 0x01AC before the suicide completes) their death purges as well, it is the run's death (`unbuff=killed`, `died=1`, `suicides=0`, a warning is logged) and the scenario goes straight to the respawn stage. The tally is not reset after the suicide: `deaths` counts both, `purged` includes the suicide's, `corpse` the death's own corpse of each, `died` only the tuskers'.
- *The death wait.* Non-combat (a mode change only if needed), `IFightWalk.BeginLeg` to the nearest tusker cell (the first leg of `DenWalk.Tour`), then the wait: the health share is logged on each change of 10%; no 0x01B2, 0x01B4 or protection notice in `IdleLegSeconds` 60 starts the next leg; the death is a 0x01AC (or NumDeaths above the suicides); `--death-seconds` bounds it, and the timeout names hits, evades, protected, damage_taken, health_min_pct and legs (D17). A null walker fails the stage (`TODO(m3-fight-no-walker)`). `DeathOptions.StopAfterHits` (an init property, not on the command line; DoD 9's short mode) stops `StopAfterHitsSeconds` = **3 s** after the first 0x01B2 with the health share under 100% (the plan's and DoD 9's "one minute" was amended by the live fix, review R6), with no re-gear and a clean log off; a death first is reported (`DiedBeforeStop`), its respawn is report-only, and the mage logs off un-geared (the next run's gear stage repairs it).
- *The respawn, the classification frozen in the world.* The scenario waits for the death's F751 and the position (60 s, else `respawn=none`), the player present, then 5 s, and classifies with `DeathLegVerdict.ClassifyRespawn`; `elsewhere` is a failure naming the arrival cell. **The scenario freezes its measures once, in the world, when it concludes or fails** (`DeathScenario.FreezeMeasures`, called by `Conclude`, by `Fail` and by the game runner's `Fail`); `Outcome(deathFrameMaxMs, fightFrameMaxMs)` reads the frozen measures and adds the game's frame figures, and `VerdictFields()` is `DeathLegVerdict`'s fields plus ` bars= unbuff= bound=`. This is the fix for a false alarm of the live rounds. The first rounds read `respawn=elsewhere` at the very end of a run whose respawn stage had read `lifestone`, and a WARN said the lifestone object "leaves the table after the respawn". **It never did:** the late re-measure ran after the log off, and `LogOffTracker` confirms the log off only at CharacterSelect with `Self.Guid == 0`, which is after `GameStateReducer.LeaveWorld` ran `objects.Clear()`, so `ClassifyRespawn` found an empty table (review R3). Our object table forgets nothing; nobody should "fix" `WorldObjectTable` or the reducer's delete gate for this. The WARN `verdict: a re-measure now would read ...` can fire only in the world (the tally's arrival overwritten, or the lifestone really gone) and is skipped out of the world.
- *The re-gear and `ReleaseDeletedHeld`.* A second `GearScenario` (`endSession: false`, no cast) spawns again what the death dropped (the mod's `item` verb: the top-up of the kit) and wields the wand and the five pieces; `gear_restored=yes` when it reads `gear=wielded worn=5`; since M4 2.2 s1 the corpse stage goes back for the mage's own corpse first (the corpse trip, below), and the re-gear spawns only what the death destroyed; `--death-recover-corpse off` is M3's run (D8). The re-gear needed no code change: `PlayerTools.SpawnGear` counts a kit item in the pack by weenie class as held and `GearScenario.Wield` wields a carried one (pinned by `GearScenarioTests.A_kit_item_already_in_the_pack_is_wielded_and_not_spawned_again_...`). The first live re-gear failed with "inventory-unsettled" (5 held items with no world object): ACE drops a wielded item with F747 and no 0x0024, and the reducer used to remove the world object but leave the guid in the equipped list. `GameStateReducer.OnDeleteObject` now calls `ReleaseDeletedHeld` (`GameStateReducer.Inventory.cs`, one line in `GameStateReducer.World.cs`), which releases a held guid with `InventoryChanged.Removed`; this closes `TODO(m3-inventory-delete-alone)`. Retail: the client takes an F747 through `CM_Physics::DispatchSB_DeleteObject` (11.6096 0x006AD600) to `SmartBox::HandleDeleteObject` (0x00451EE0), then `ACCObjectMaint::DeleteObject` (0x00558330) and `CObjectMaint::DeleteObject` (0x00508FA0), which destroys the physics object and the weenie object whatever container or slot held it; **`TODO(m3-inventory-delete-retail)`** (logged once, with the first release): whether the weenie's destruction hook also takes the item out of retail's placement list was not read. The scenario's `regear-settle` stage logs held items with no world object (guid and held kind, container, placement, location) and, after `RegearSettleSeconds` 5, fails naming their guids.
- *The decoys (M4 3.1 d0, D20 and D22).* The death loop's kit is `GearKit.ForDeathLoop(spells, account, options)`: the plain `GearKit.Build` kit plus the eight `DecoyKit` lines (`GearKind.Decoy`: one item each, no valid location, never wielded), which `--death-decoys on|off` (default on; off is M3's kit exactly) switches. ACE drops the highest-valued items of a death (`Player_Death.cs` 481-603 and 629-673, `DeathItem.cs`: level 126 drops 6 to 8 items), so the decoys are ordinary, light items worth 8,000 or more, each the first of its death category (Massive Mana Charge 27329, Intricate Carving Tool 9295, Handle Mold 41396, Tusker Island Shirt 22565, Skull Cap 25522, Virindi Scalpel 11007, Healing Chocolate Olthoi Cake 11129, Entrancing Adjanite Gem 27771; 452 burden; four sold by vendors in the pinned world database), and they outrank what the loops need to kill the tuskers: the wand (The Healer's Heart 8552, 5,400), the Olthoi Helm, the Platemail Gauntlets and the Sollerets, and every spell component but the Mana Scarab (level VIII spells only; carried, 15,000 each, one more item above the wand). Bonded gear (the Greater Amuli Shadow Coat and Leggings, the Platinum Scarab) is never a candidate. `DeathDropModel` is ACE's rule as a pure function (category by ItemType, candidates, per-unit stack value, the halving of every item after the first of its category, the variance as bounds) and `Survives` says whether the needed items can be among the dropped: at least N_max not-needed candidates must have a minimum final value above the best needed item's maximum (unhalved, +10%). `DecoyKit.Check(state, level)` runs it over the live inventory once per gear stage and returns the one log line (`decoy check: N items above the wand ...`) and the `decoy_safe` field's value (`DecoyCheck.Field`). The count is `CountFor(level) = min(8, MaxDrops(level))`: the decoys alone cover N_max and the Mana Scarab is the spare. The gear scenario spawns a missing decoy by the same `item` top-up (counted by weenie class, one each; `GearScenario.DecoyGrants`), never wields one, and for a decoy kit `kit=<held>/<count>` counts them (`gear_steps` stays the wand's and the armour's). Tests: `DecoyKitTests`, `DeathDropModelTests` (an independent simulation of ACE's draw agrees), `DeathDecoyOptionsTests`, and the decoy rows of `GearKitTests` and `GearScenarioTests`. TODOs: `TODO(m4-decoy-bonded-flag)` (the weenie description carries no bonded flag; the check takes the kit's three bonded weenies, `DeathDropModel.BondedKitWcids`, as bonded and every other item as not bonded) and `TODO(m4-decoy-value-unknown)` (an item whose description sends no Value counts as 0, as ACE's `wo.Value ?? 0`). The decoy check's line ends with `TODO(m4-decoy-bonded-flag): the bonded gate rests on the kit's three known bonded weenies, any other bonded item counts as a candidate` every time, and with `; TODO(m4-decoy-value-unknown): N held object(s) sent no Value and count as 0` when a description sent no Value (counted as 0, as ACE's `wo.Value ?? 0`). Full line: `decoy check: 9 items above the wand (best case 5940, the weakest of the 9 at least 7200); 6 to 8 drop at level 126 of 15 candidates: safe; TODO(m4-decoy-bonded-flag): ...`. After the decoy check the gear stage logs the live load: `load: <burden> of <capacity> burden, <p>% of the capacity` (the inventory page's `EncumbranceSystem.Load`, as `LootRules` reads it), and warns over 95% (`DecoyKit.LoadWarnPercent`): 'the decoy kit and duplicates leave the mage at N% of its capacity'. No behaviour changes. The regear before the trip spawns every kit item a death dropped and the trip returns the other, so duplicates are carried; letting the regear skip what the corpse will return needs the listing before the regear and is a design question left open.
- *The bars on the screen.* The panel shots open the first tab that holds spells (`docs/retail-ui.md` section 23.8); play keeps retail's first tab.
- *Headless.* `HeadlessScenario.DeathAndRegear` (section 20): summary line `death <fields>` before `headless done`, the default time limit 2,310 s with the defaults (900 + 630 + `--death-fight-seconds` + `--death-seconds`, section 20).
- *Tests.* `DeathScenarioTests` (scripted ACE in ACE's order, the stand-in for the gear and re-gear; the unbuff race, a respawn elsewhere, the missing-object failure and the freeze), `DeathTallyTests`, `DeathLegVerdictTests`, `DeathTrackerTests`, `DeathActionCodecTests`, `DeathSendTests`, `WorldObjectTableFlagQueryTests`, `HeadlessDeathOptionsTests`, `LoginCompleteWaitTests` (the closed F747 gap). M4 2.2 s1 adds the corpse trip's tests: `DeathScenarioTests` (the partial `DeathScenarioTests.Corpse.cs`: the happy path, each skip, each abort, a death on the trip, the frozen fields), `DeathLegVerdictTests`, `DeathCorpseOptionsTests`, `DeathLegRunnerTests` (the fifth slot) and `GearScenarioTests` (a kit item in the pack).

**The loopback tests (the lead only; `[LoopbackFact]`, `DeathLive.Run` in `DeathLiveTests.cs`).** `IncomingDamageLiveTests.The_unbuffed_mage_among_the_tuskers_takes_a_blow_and_its_health_share_falls` (DoD 9): `StopAfterHits`; `hits_taken >= 1`, `damage_taken > 0`, `health_min_pct < 100`. `DeathLiveTests.The_mage_dies_to_the_tuskers_respawns_at_Holtburgs_lifestone_with_vitae_and_is_regeared` (DoD 10): `died == 1`, `vitae > 0`, `respawn == lifestone`, the death's own corpse (`corpse >= 1`, review R1), `gear_restored`, `purged >= 1` when it suicided, the vitals between 60% and 90%, hits and damage above 0. What each proves is the DoD line it names; neither is part of `slice-check`. `DeathLiveTests` runs `DeathStages.All`, so it includes the trip since M4 2.2 s1; it prints `death_live_corpse ...` and asserts nothing about the corpse fields (a skipped or aborted trip is a legitimate live outcome). M4 3.1 d2 adds the decoy loop's assertions to the same test: the old DoD 10 assertions read the FROZEN verdict fields where the fight and the trip could move a live tally (`died`, `vitals_pct`, `hits_taken`, `damage_taken`), and `decoy_safe=yes`, `gear_dropped=0`, the corpse's listing at least 6 entries (level/20) with `decoys_dropped >= listed - 1` (a flat `decoys_dropped >= 6` fails one run in three by design: the unneeded Mana Scarab, 15,000, ranks among the top drops and N is 6, 7 or 8), `decoys_recovered == decoys_dropped`, `rebuffed=yes` and `den_kills >= 1`; a field that is n/a fails the test naming it and the trip's state. It prints `death_live_decoys ...`. The ceiling for the headless run is 2,310 s (900 + 630 + 180 + 600); expected 10 to 15 minutes.

**The corpse trip (M4 2.2 s1, D5).** `--death-recover-corpse on|off` (default on) and `--death-recover-min-health PCT` (default 60). Preconditions (each failing one is `corpse_looted=skipped` and a Warn line "corpse: skipped (corpse_looted=skipped): <reason>"): respawn classified `lifestone`; a corpse remembered (`DeathTally.LastCorpseGuid`, `LastCorpsePosition`, `LastCorpseAt`: the F745's cell, position and session clock, kept because the table forgets the corpse after the teleport away); under `PlayerCorpseMinRotSeconds` = 3600 s since the F745 (`W/Corpse.cs:103-118`: `max(3600, level x 300)` s with items, 15 s empty); and a health share at least the minimum. The trip: `PlayerTools.Place` (`place loc`) to the remembered cell and position, `CorpseSettleSeconds` = 1 s, the den's floor rule, the corpse by guid within 15 s, `OpenContainer`, `CorpseLooter` with `LootRules.TakeAll`, `CloseContainer`, then `Place` back to Holtburg's point (the bind's) and a 3 s settle. Each abort is named and counted as `trip_abort=`: `health-under-40`, `trip-over-90s`, `corpse-not-found`, `open-refused` (an open view is closed fire-and-forget; an open that lands late is closed too), `off-floor`, `place-refused` (nothing placed back: the mage never left). A death on the trip (a 0x01AC, the Dead motion or a NumDeaths rise) fails the leg with the corpse's state named (`trip_abort=died`). **The protection does not survive the trip:** ACE dispels the lifestone protection on every teleport of the player (`Player_Location.cs` 702-703), so the trip's departure ends it and the arrival among the tuskers is unprotected. The trip is kept short instead: with the fight on it comes after the regear, the rebuff and the fight (the tuskers are dead when it arrives; M4 3.1 d1); with the fight off it starts at the end of the respawn's vitals wait. The settle is 1 s, and the 40% abort applies. The first trip also makes `protection=dispelled` true in the tally, so M3's judged fields are frozen: the tally-derived measures (`died deaths vitae corpse purged protected hits_taken damage_taken evades health_min_pct corpses_stale protection`) are taken once at the end of `respawn-vitals` (`_atRespawn`, now for every run, not only one with a trip), and the regear, the rebuff, the fight and the trip never move them; a death during the rebuff or the fight fails the leg naming the stage (`rebuff: the mage died during the rebuff ...`, `fight: ...`, never a second `died=1` count); the health precondition (60%) now meets the mage after the fight, so a fight that leaves it under 60% skips the trip (`TODO(m4-death-trip-health)`) unless the pre-trip heal below brings it back; the trip's blows, teleports and re-created old corpses never move them.

**The corpse's listing (M4 3.1 d1).** The opened corpse's own entries are kept (guid, then weenie class as the F745s arrive) and classified when the trip turns back: `decoys_dropped=` (listed `DecoyKit.IsDecoy` entries), `gear_dropped=` (listed entries `DeathDropModel.IsNeeded` says the loops need), `decoys_recovered=` (the listed decoys that are in the pack after the trip: a taken entry keeps its guid). With the fight on, the regear ran BEFORE the trip, so a needed item that was listed and not taken is a Warn line, never a failure (`corpse: N item(s) the loops need were on the corpse and were not taken`; report-only in its first gate, as every new judge is, CLAUDE.md); the leg line's `gear_left=<n|n/a>` counts the listed items the loops need that the trip did not take from the corpse (n/a when no listing was read). In M4 2.2's order the regear after the trip covers it and the run only warns. Because the regear runs BEFORE the trip, what is left behind is a duplicate on the corpse, not a missing item. With `--death-decoys off` every decoy field is `n/a` and nothing is classified.

**The pre-trip heal (M4 3.1 d1 F1; `DeathScenario.Heal.cs`).** With the fight on, when the health share is the ONLY failing trip precondition the stage `pretrip-heal` heals first: Heal Self VII (2073, `ArenaProfile.FightExtras`) through `IActionApi.Cast`, at most 4 casts (each logged: the share before, the outcome, the share after; a cast is skipped when the mana is under `GearCasts.ManaCeiling` or the spell is not in the SpellTable, and an outcome other than a success or a fizzle ends the casting), then up to 60 s of regeneration on the session clock (logged on each 10%). A mage still under `--death-recover-min-health` skips the trip as before (`TODO(m4-death-trip-health)`). The fight scenario leaves the mage in non-combat and a cast needs the magic combat mode (the client guard, D40), so the stage enters the magic mode before its first cast and leaves it after. Not in M4 2.2's order. The verdict line gains `pretrip_heals=<n>` (report-only).

**The unbuff top-up (M4 3.1 d1 F2; `DeathScenario.TopUp.cs`).** The unbuff's suicide is a death too and drops items by the same rule, and the new order ends every run buffed, so after the suicide's respawn (`unbuff-vitals`) the stage `unbuff-decoys` spawns the decoys the pack lacks with the `item` verb (`PlayerTools.SpawnGear` over a kit of the decoys alone: counted by weenie class, nothing wielded, no cast), waits for them as the gear stage does, and runs `DecoyKit.Check` again, so `decoy_safe=` is read after the top-up and `decoys=` (counted when the wait among the tuskers begins) after it too. The suicide's own corpse stays in the den holding the old decoys and is not gone back for (the trip targets the death's own corpse by the tally's window); one log line says so. Skipped without a suicide, with `--death-decoys off` and for a kit without decoys; the `unbuff=killed` race has no top-up (the tuskers' kill is the run's death). Open: `m4-death-trip-heal-resume` (a heal skipped for mana does not resume when the mana regenerates during the wait).

**The leg: `--login-check=bsd-death`.** A kind of its own, in the style of section 31: never part of `--login-check=all`, hooked by one line (`DeathLegRunner.Install`) in `LoginCheckRunner.Install`. The game enters BSD Mage on the test account (absent: it fails loudly, "run --scenario=mage-build"), waits for the arrival (Travel Present, 3 s settle) and runs `DeathScenario` in the game's session with `DeathStages.All` and `endSession: false`, then logs off and quits. A failure at any stage logs off first. A bad `--death-seconds` fails the leg at its first frame ("bad death option: ..."). The runner parses `DeathLegOptions` (Client, `Session/Scenarios/DeathLegOptions.cs`): `DeathOptions` (the five options `--death-decoys`, `--death-rebuff`, `--death-fight`, `--death-fight-kills`, `--death-fight-seconds` and the older ones), `BuffOptions.FromArguments` (the `--buff-*` options: the rebuff is a buff cycle) and a `FightOptions` whose `Kills` and `Seconds` are the death options' (the fight leg's own `--fight-*` are not the death loop's); a bad value in any of them fails the leg at its first frame, as the others do. The kit is `GearKit.ForDeathLoop` (the decoys with `--death-decoys on`, M3's plain kit with off).
- *The game's player is both walkers.* `GameFightWalk` (the den, section 31) and `GameApproachWalk` (`scripts/Session/GameApproachWalk.cs`: the walk from the Holtburg place to within the lifestone's use radius before the Use) share one `RoutePlayHost`; `GameApproachWalk` drives the player controller with a `RouteDriver` over a one-goal `ScriptedRoute`, as `HoltburgApproach` does headless, and refuses an unplaced body.
- *The loading worker* reads the SpellTable, builds the gear kit (the login name hashed inside the worker), loads the den's floor graph and prepares the methods of the `Scenarios` and `Mage` namespaces, `GameFightWalk`, `GameApproachWalk`, `DeathLegRunner` and `RouteDriver` (the log line "the death loop's methods were prepared on the loading worker"). The session's own handlers for the death's messages (0x01AC, the vitae, 0x02C6, the corpse) are prepared by `SessionJitWarmUp` at start-up.
- *The frame measures.* `DeathFrameSampler` (Client; no allocation): `death_frame_max_ms`, the worst frame from the player's own Dead motion (F74C) to the first frame after the player is present again at the death's arrival, **portal space and the arrival's landblock load included**: the frame the motion was seen in counts from the next call's delta; the arrival (the self teleport after the death's F751) is counted, not waited for, since ACE sends the F751 and that position in one call (`Player_Location.cs` 686-696); the call that saw `SelfTravel.Present` counts (its delta is the last portal-space frame) and two calls after it (the frame the player materialised in and the first frame after it) close the window (package 2 review, finding 1: a window closed three calls after the position missed the tunnel and the materialise burst); each death (the suicide's, then the tuskers') opens one; the frame after a saved review frame is left out. It also keeps `CorpseBeforePortal` (the corpse's CreateObject between the Dead motion and the F751) and the arrivals, for the review frames, with the corpse window of R1. Report-only (a new judge, plan section 5; the lead decides when it is judged against 50 ms). `fight_frame_max_ms` is `FightFrameSampler`'s with the den stages (`den-settle`, `unbuff`, `unbuff-dying`, `death-mode`, `death-wait`) as "fighting" and the target always engaged; report-only here.
- *The detail line.* `bsd-death detail seconds= stage= stage_timings= unbuff= bars= bound= legs= dead_motions= death_portals= death_arrivals= corpses_seen= corpse_before_portal= death_windows= death_frames= worst_death_frame_at_s= worst_death_frame_ms= worst_death_frame_stage= worst_death_session_tick_ms= ... worst_death_play_mode_events_applied= fight_frames= worst_fight_frame_at_s= worst_fight_frame_ms= ... review_frames= scenario_verdict=` (the frame attribution of the slowest death frame and of the slowest den frame, the stage timings as `stage@run-seconds`, the review frames). The leg line is `DeathLegVerdict`'s, with d1's fields after `trip_abort=` in the order above, then `gear_left=<n|n/a>` after `pretrip_heals=`, then `mode_mismatch=<n>` (report-only; printed before the optional `frame_over_50=yes`, as on the hunt leg line: `... pretrip_heals=<n> gear_left=<n|n/a> mode_mismatch=<n>[ frame_over_50=yes]`; `DeathLegVerdict.WithField`, `DeathLegRunner.LegLine`). The stage timings include the new stages by name, as `NoteStage` appends every stage the scenario enters.
- *The review frames.* With `--play-screenshot=PATH` and no `--play-screenshot-after` (`PlayOptions.Parse` moves the path to `BuffShotPath` for `bsd-death`), six 800x600 frames with the developer HUD hidden, `DeathShotPlan` (Client, next to `FightShotPlan`): `<stem>-hit.png` (a 0x01B2 with the health under 100%, plus 0.2 s: the vitals down), `<stem>-dead.png` (the player's Dead motion plus 1.5 s: the death line in the chat), `<stem>-corpse.png` (the corpse's F745 plus 0.3 s) and `<stem>-lifestone.png` (the death's arrival plus 3.5 s, taken at the first frame with the player present: the lifestone and the 75% vitals). `<stem>-corpse-open.png` (M4 2.2 s1, `DeathShotPlan` slot 4 `corpse-open`): the mage's own corpse open, 0.5 s after the scenario lists it, with the scenario held (at most 3 s) so nothing is taken yet and the container window (lane s2) shows the contents. It is not named `corpse`, because that slot (the death-time corpse, always given up live by ACE's one-tick corpse and teleport) already owns `<stem>-corpse.png`. A run with no trip reads `corpse-open=trigger never came`. The plan watches from the moment the scenario waits among the tuskers (so the unbuff suicide's Dead motion, corpse and arrival are not the frames; on `unbuff=killed` the plan is armed on the victim notice, usually after the Dead motion in the same pump, and the dead, corpse and lifestone frames read `trigger never came`); each slot is triggered once; the scenario is held at most 3 s after a trigger while a frame is owed (the lifestone's 3.5 s runs past the hold). A slot whose trigger never came reads `trigger never came`. **The corpse frame is given up by name and never faked:** ACE creates the corpse and sends the teleport in one tick, so portal space is up 0.3 s after the corpse's F745 (`not taken (portal space was already up 0.3 s after the corpse's F745)`; the log says which came first), and the world is not drawn in portal space. The corpse is proved by the tally (R1's window) and, drawn in the Dead pose, by the offline loop. The frames are derived from the retail dats and stay under `cache/`. In the live frames the vitae page is open by itself in the dead and lifestone frames: that is **retail** (`gmVitaeUI::RecvNotice_VitaeChanged`, 11.6096 0x004A7AA0, opens page 15 when the multiplier falls while the page is hidden; `docs/retail-ui.md` section 23.3), and the red clipped "In" at the vitals' left is the spew box's "In Portal Space - Please Wait..." row under the dats' vitals window, 0.1 s after the tunnel ended (whether retail clears it at the tunnel's end is open). M4 3.1 d2 adds `<stem>-decoys.png` (`DeathShotPlan` slot 5 `decoys`): the mage's inventory page open with the decoys listed. It is watched BEFORE the plan is armed for the tuskers' death (the slot's trigger is read even before `Begin`): once the gear stage's decoy check has been made (`DeathScenario.DecoyCheckResult`; the scenario is then in its `bars` stage), the runner opens the inventory page with the panel host's own toggle (`GamePanelHost.Toggle(GameInventoryPage.Panel)`, what the toolbar button does; never a session action), reads the decoys the main pack holds, and the frame is due 0.3 s after the open (two hidden frames at least, the scenario held at most 3 s) and only while every one of those decoys has its cell in the page's pack list (the list scrolled with `ScrollToShow` to the first of them, and three frames drawn). The slot is given up by name after 3 s (`the inventory page did not list the decoys within 3.0 s`), and the page is closed again when the frame is taken or given up. A shell with no inventory page within 8 s of the check, or `--death-decoys off` (no check), leaves the slot reading `decoys=trigger never came`: the frame is never faked. `corpse-open` keeps slot 4 and its trigger; the trip is now last, after the fight (the plan is armed at the wait among the tuskers, as before).
- *Limits.* `DeathLegRunner.WholeCheckSecondsFor(deathSeconds, fightSeconds = 180)` = login 60 + gear 120 + spell bars 30 + bind 90 + suicide and respawn 90 + the decoy top-up 30 + the den 30 + `--death-seconds` (default 600) + respawn 65 + re-gear 120 + the rebuff 240 (the fight leg's buff top-up limit; a live cycle of 26 casts took 56 s) + the teleport back to the den 30 + the walk to the tuskers 60 + `--death-fight-seconds` (default 180, counted from the first cast) + the pre-trip heal 120 (four casts at 15 s and 60 s of regeneration) + the corpse trip 90 + the return to Holtburg 60 = 2,015 s (M3's 1,205 s plus 630 s of new stages plus the fight's 180 s); the limit is always the whole loop, a stage that is off only finishes early; a log off after a failure may take 120 s more (`RecoverySeconds`). The smoke step gives the Godot run 2500 s (a test pins `2500 >= 2015 + 120 + 300` and each limit against the scenario's own stage limits).
- *Run it live* (the lead only, backend running, BSD Mage built): `pwsh -File tools\smoke.ps1 -Backend require -Only login-death`, or with the frames `pwsh -File tools\godot.ps1 -Run -Offscreen -IsolateUserData -TimeoutSeconds 2500 -Resolution 800x600 -GameArgs:'--account=test --login-check=bsd-death --play-screenshot=cache/m3/3-2/death.png'`. It changes the character: vitae, dropped items, a corpse in the den.

**The smoke step `login-death`** (`tools/smoke.ps1`): `--account=test --login-check=bsd-death`, 2500 s, a backend step run ONLY under `-Backend require` (SKIP under `auto` and `start`: it changes the character), after `login-bsd` in `$backendSteps`. Its judge is `bsd-death done ok=true` and exit code 0, and it prints the verdict line. It copies into its summary, report-only (`n/a` when a field is absent): `corpse_looted items_recovered trip_s trip_abort rebuffed den_kills decoys decoy_safe decoys_dropped gear_dropped decoys_recovered pretrip_heals mode_mismatch`; its judge is unchanged. The step's help text lists the fields and the six frames. `slice-check` never runs it.

**The whole loop offline** (`PlayChecks.Death.cs`, after db's own check, section 16). `DeathScenario` runs against db's stand-in tusker (a second one, guid 0x80000C04, attacking, placed when the scenario waits) and a second stand-in lifestone (0x80000C03, Used by the check as db's is). "Arena here" (no teleports): the `Gear`, `Bind` and `Den` stages do not run (the bind is the check's own Use); `Death` (a still walk, `StandInStillWalk`: the tusker is in reach) and `Regear` run, plus `Unbuff` only when the stand-in's player holds no enchantment (it holds one in the current visit, so it is left out: the stage subscribes its tally only in a place or death stage, so the suicide path needs the teleports). Judged by `DeathScenario.Outcome` = `DeathLegVerdict.Compose` (`died=1 respawn=lifestone gear_restored=yes hits_taken>=1 damage_taken>0`) and five more checks of the death on screen; reported: `death_frame_max_ms`, `fight_frame_max_ms`. **The protected blow and the corpse drawn are offline only** (the protected-blow check and the corpse check of `PlayChecks.Death.cs`), because ACE dispels the protection on the teleport; DoD 10's audit row names which evidence is live and which is offline. M4 3.1 d2: the arena-here run names its stages and its options (`Decoys = false, Rebuff = false, Fight = false`; the stages `Death | Regear` plus `Unbuff` as before, no `Gear`, `Bind`, `Den`, `Corpse`, `Rebuff` or `Fight`): the new order's stages have no teleports, no inventory to spawn into and no real den offline (D23: no creature is added to the stand-in), so they are proved by `DeathScenarioTests` over scripted rigs and live by the lead.
- *The death on screen, checked on the stand-in with no fix needed.* The player's Dead motion (the server's F74C, stance NonCombat, forward Dead) reaches the body through `PhysicsPlayerBody.ApplyServerMovement` (`CPhysics::SetObjectMovement`, 11.6096 0x0050A160) and plays (`Substate = Dead 0x40000011` within a frame; the Ready of the respawn stands it up, `Ready 0x41000003`; `PlayerIsDead`, 11.6096 0x006B4CB0, already stops the player's own keys while the forward command is Dead). The corpse is an ordinary server object (`BF_CORPSE`, "Corpse of <name>", the player's Setup and motion table, forward Dead): its own avatar, drawn by the server-objects layer in the Dead pose; the player's body does nothing with its CreateObject (the ObjDesc is the stand-in's placeholder, `TODO(m3-standin-corpse-weenie)`; whether ACE's corpse wears the player's clothing is first seen live). Portal space plays on the death's F751 like any teleport's. `GameVitals` follows the 0x02E9 updates: 0.00 at the death, 0.75 and 0.74 after the arrival. The test `the-player-plays-the-dead-motion-on-its-own-body-and-stands-up-after-the-respawn` pins the first (`docs/avatars.md` section 17 has the avatar side).

**Live facts (the lead's rounds, 2026-10-17; the bundled ACE).** The bind took with the attune line seen (one round in 5.7 s), the approach walk bringing the player from 5.4 to 6.6 m away to 0.8 to 2.5 m of the lifestone. The unbuff suicide purged (`purged=1`). Unbuffed among the Tusker Slaves the mage was hit (3 to 7 hits, 56 to 135 damage; evades too) and **died in 11 to 22 s** (`death_s`; its maximum health was 174), the protection having been dispelled by the teleport (`protected=0` in every round). The respawn was at Holtburg's lifestone through portal space (`respawn=lifestone`), the vitals 75.6 to 76.5% three seconds after the arrival, `gear_restored=yes worn=5` with the kit 59/59. **The vitae climbed 5% a death and reached ACE's 40% cap** (D35; at the cap a 1% step costs about 35,000 XP, a kill gives 30,000): BSD Mage is at the cap and stays near it. The leg's last live run: `bsd-death ok=true died=1 vitae=40 respawn=lifestone vitals_pct=75.6 gear_restored=yes worn=5 hits_taken=3 damage_taken=78 death_s=21 death_frame_max_ms=11.0 fight_frame_max_ms=13.4` (an earlier run read 16.7 ms); the frames hit, dead and lifestone were taken and the corpse frame was given up by name. Every earlier `respawn=elsewhere`, "inventory-unsettled" and no-UseDone failure is one of the fixes above (the attune line, the approach walk, `ReleaseDeletedHeld`, the freeze). The live gate (the worst of three, the full smoke) is the lead's and not recorded here.

**Open TODO names of 3.2.** Opened: `m3-purge-empty` (an empty purge publishes nothing: the reducer, the lead's call), `m3-lifestone-radius`, `m3-bind-approach`, `m3-death-walk-replace` (only if the walker is not re-placed after the suicide's teleport; not seen live), `m3-inventory-delete-retail`, `m3-standin-bars-live`, the stand-in's own (`m3-standin-tusker-attack`, `m3-standin-vitae-spell`, `m3-standin-instantiation`, `m3-standin-lifestone-weenie`, `m3-standin-corpse-weenie`, `m3-standin-death-items`) and the windows' (`docs/retail-ui.md` section 23.8). Closed: `m3-death-leg` (dd), `m3-standin-death` (db), `m3-standin-corpse` for the player (db; a creature's still makes none), `m3-inventory-delete-alone` (da fix 4) and `retailui-runtime` for the three combat windows. All in `docs/milestones/m3-plan.md` section 9.

**Open TODO names of 3.1 (the death loop, M4 3.1 d0 to d2).** `TODO(m4-decoy-bonded-flag)` and `TODO(m4-decoy-value-unknown)` (the decoys); `TODO(m4-death-trip-health)` (mostly answered by the pre-trip heal: a mage still under the minimum skips the trip) and `TODO(m4-death-trip-heal-resume)`. Closed in 3.1 d1 F2: `m4-death-unbuff-decoys`. Named, not TODO: the headless host's `Program.DeathQuitAfterOverheadSeconds` (900) and its test `HeadlessDeathOptionsTests` still say the 2.2 loop's overhead (a CLI default only: the live test passes its own limit from `DeathLive.QuitAfterSecondsFor`), and whether the fight's frames join the death leg's report-only `fight_frame_max_ms` (`InDenStage` lists only the unbuffed den stages) is the lead's call.

## 33. The M3 4.2 polish pass (D39-D43)

The end of M3: the owner's polish items P1 to P5 and the 4.2 audit fixes, each a slice (`cache/m3/4-2/`). The prose here is the
one place for the slices' notes; the sections above name the behaviour where it lives.

- **P1, the cast bar (p1).** The gear stage of the death loop binds BSD Mage's bars: tab I (ACE bar 0) is Focus, Willpower and
