  Creature Enchantment Mastery at VI, then the 24 buff lines at VII in `BuffCycle.OrderedLines` order; tab II (bar 1) is Fire
  Vulnerability Other VII (2170), Flame Bolt VII (2128), Revitalize Self VII (2083), Stamina to Mana Self VII (2345) and Heal Self
  VII (2073); tab III keeps whatever ACE holds. The build learns 2170 and 2073 too (`ArenaProfile.FightExtras`; 249 spells).
  `login-buff` runs the bars step after the gear (`MageSpellBars`; log only).
- **P2, the target panel (p2).** Section 21.7 has the blank-panel rule and the auto-target rule.
- **P3, the stance rules (p3).** Section 23.8 has the combat-mode refusals and the held-item rule. The cast guard is in §7.
- **P4, the wall shots (p4).** Section 31 has the pick, the wall-hit rule and the frames.
- **P5, the testing loop (p5a, p5b).** The default filter is `Category!=Loopback&Tier!=Soak&Tier!=Exhaustive&Tier!=Timing&Status!=KnownFailure` (M4 1.1 s3; README.md,
  tests/Shared/README.md, CLAUDE.md's own copy is the owner's). `slice-check`'s `-MinTests` defaults to the baseline file `tests/Shared/test-count-baseline.json`,
  and the exhaustive stage is floored by that file's `exhaustive` count. `slice-check -Exhaustive` (or `-Stages exhaustive`) runs the
  `Tier=Exhaustive` tests (76, the dat sweeps: `RenderSurface`, `SceneryOracle`, `SceneryWorldSweep` with the constant test,
  `CellVisibilityExhaustive`, `ModelsExhaustive`). The Exhaustive tier of `Lifestone.Data.Tests` takes 69 s, not 92 s. The
  script-side cuts: G-A is `slice-check -Smoke -MinTests <baseline>`, which implies `-Exhaustive`, `-SoloFrames` and `-FullScan`
  and no longer runs `dotnet test` twice; `smoke.ps1 -Backend require` implies `-SoloFrames` and `-FullScan` and prints
  `frames: solo (-SoloFrames implied by -Backend require)` on its first line (the probe and the hero takes still need
  `-SoloFrames` named); a `--play-frames` run is `--play-checks --play-skip-scenarios --play-frames` (`--play-skip-scenarios`
  is registered in `OptionCatalog.Play.cs`). The test-side cuts: `IncomingDamageLiveTests` is dropped (its one unique assert,
  `health_min_pct < 100`, is in `DeathLiveTests`); the eight mod-verb loopback tests are three (`The_status_places_and_refusal_verbs_...`,
  `The_place_verbs_refuse_busy_...`, `The_spell_learn_and_item_verbs_...`; a failure message starts `[verb]`); the idle test is
  `Idling_in_the_character_list_past_the_silence_watchdog_does_not_trip_it` and idles 30 s (the 20 s watchdog plus 10 s), not 45 s.
  The death wait of about 53 s in `DeathScenario` is not the tusker's rhythm: the player arrives under ACE's lifestone protection
  (`Player_Death.cs` 907-933; dispelled only by the player's own actions, 935-940), and the stand-in absorbs the blows; unprotected,
  the same tusker killed the player in 8.9 s. `TODO(q5-death-wait-protection)` names it. The stand-in's tusker rhythm stays
  `TODO(m3-standin-tusker-attack)`; the live per-hit damage is 35 to 40 against the placeholder's 8 to 16.
- **The death loop's record (p5b).** `DeathOptions.StopAfterHits` has no caller after the cut and is a candidate for removal
  (review R11).
- **The stand-in's humans (p6).** The stand-in sends a ModelData for every human-Setup object and the player (`StandInVisit.HumanModel`,
  `HumanLooks`); the corpse copies the player's model (ACE `Creature_Death.cs` 474-480). The host composes the looks through
  `CharGenAppearance.Compose` (`PlayChecks.HumanLooks.cs`); every garment is a placeholder (`TODO(m3-standin-human-look)`, logged
  once per role). The live path applies ObjDescs correctly (a replay of both recordings, no appearance problems); only the stand-in
  sent none. The stand-in sends no F625 on a wield, an unwield or the death's lost items (`TODO(m3-standin-objdesc-on-wield)`; ACE
  `Creature_Equipment.cs` 365, 438). The report-only play-check field `humans_dressed=n/m` counts humans with at least one
  texture-changed part over the humans the stand-in made.
- **The texture fallback (fix A).** A texture change whose new texture cannot load keeps the surface's own texture (retail
  `CSurface::UseTextureMap` 11.6096 0x00537020 not applied; `CPartArray::SetTextureMap` 0x00519110 reports the failure and the part
  draws); counted in `TextureLoader.OverrideFallbacks` and told once per texture (the "textures" warning, `override-unloadable`).
  Before this, the loader threw and the whole part's mesh was dropped. It is retail's rule, not a glitch fix, so no flag.
- **Smoke, judges and the record (x3).** A step with `frame_over_50=yes` reads `PASS (frame over 50 ms)`; its detail ends
  `; frame_over_50=<yes|no> <window>_frame_max_ms=<x> ...`. The record-audio levels line ends `windows=<n> clipped_samples=<n>[ muted=...]`
  (report-only). The soak log prints `soak counts: client CRC failures N, client duplicate server packets dropped N, link duplicate
  encrypted deliveries C2S N S2C N` beside the ACE-side counts (`LivePhaseBTests`). The pinned NotInM3 set (`GamePanelsTests`) is panel
  ids 1, 2, 8, 9, 12, 14, 16 and 25.
- **The shell's first paint (fix B).** A step already over 2 ms while the shell holds the world skips its paint, and a step over 6 ms
  after its paint skips the upload (`RetailUiLayer.ShellBusyMilliseconds` and `ShellCopySkipMilliseconds`). The one remaining spike was a single
  gen1 collection of 4 to 8 ms inside one element's construction in the first (long) shell build (`GamePlayScreen`'s staged build). M4 1.1 s4 holds
  the collector off over that build with `GC.TryStartNoGCRegion(240 MiB)` (now `NoGcWindow`, ended in `Complete`, `Dispose` or a throwing build, by its owner only;
  off under the test host through an internal seam, not a command-line option). The build allocates about 11 MiB on the game thread. 24 MiB was too small
  under server GC (the window ended early and the gen1 still ran: the budget is per heap); 240 MiB held. The build's log line gained `staged unit gc <ms>
  (<gen0, gen1, gen2>)`, `wiring unit gc <ms>`, `build gc <ms> gen0/1/2 a/b/c` and `no-gc window held|ended early|refused|off (start <ms>, allocated <KiB>)`;
  the play-check `no-ui-step-after-enter-is-over-12-ms` detail gained `shell_gc_pause_ms_gen012=<ms>/<gen0>/<gen1>/<gen2>` (report-only).
  Before (master): longest unit 8.5 ms, worst_ui_step 9.9 to 12.4 ms (one run failed the 12 ms rule). After: longest unit 2.4 ms, worst_ui_step 7.6 ms, GC 0.
  Live probe (the lead, solo, `smoke.ps1 -Only login-check,login -Backend require -SoloFrames`, one sample each): before, login-check's shell longest frame
  4.5 ms and longest unit 2.1 ms, login's longest frame 9.8 ms and longest unit 8.9 ms with a GC in it; after, login-check 4.6 ms and 2.2 ms, login 4.3 ms and
  2.4 ms, build gc 0/0/0, the no-GC window held (10,310 and 14,436 KiB allocated). `TODO(m3-ui-step-gc-margin)` stays open: its closure waits on the post-window number (the first collection after the window ends: pause, generation and frames after `Complete`, in the log line "first collection after the no-gc window" and the play-check detail `shell_post_window_gc`); it closes only if that collection is also under the 12 ms margin. The window is sized from the GC heap count (`NoGcWindow`: 32 MiB a heap, 240 MiB at least, 512 MiB at most, so it holds up to 16 heaps and says UNDERSIZED beyond that).
- **Engine notes (fix B).** `RenderingServer.GlobalShaderParameterGetList()` logs an engine ERROR outside the editor: never call it.
  `DynamicLightTable.Declare` adds the sampler without asking.
- **The hero video's live takes (h).** The capture, edit and measure scripts for the M3 hero video are under `cache/m3/4-2/hero`; see
  [hero-video.md](hero-video.md) §8.
- **The loop's log lines (h).** The legs' log lines the hero video uses as beat anchors: the cast landed, the cast confirmed (the
  projectile), the fight is won, the death (the tuskers killed the player) and the regear done.

## 34. The fighter (M4 1.1; D1, D9, D13, D14; DoD 2)

M4's fights need a character that fights in melee and at range and heals itself: `Holtburg Hunter`, an Aluvian on the test account, built to level 40 by a
scenario. The mage (section 29) is the pattern; the code is the reference and this section says where things are.

**The template** (`Session/Fighter/FighterTemplate.cs`, with `FighterSpendingPlanner`; `FighterTemplateDatTests`, which pins the numbers against the dats and fails when
the rule changes). The creation intent (`CreationIntent(name, retailClasses)`): Aluvian male; Strength, Endurance and Coordination 100, Quickness, Focus and Self 10 (330
of the attribute points). Four skills are trained at creation: Heavy Weapons (44) **specialised** (12 credits), Missile Weapons (47) 6, Healing (21) 6 and Melee Defense (6) 10:
34 of the heritage's 52 credits, 18 carried forward unspent. Level 40 is 20,502,261 XP (`XpTable.XpForLevel(40)`) and 20 skill credits through level 40
(`SkillCreditsThroughLevel(40, 1)`), so 38 credits are left unspent. No skill is trained later (`TrainedLater` is empty).

The plan's targets (`Targets`: ranks above the creation value, in plan order; each takes its share of the level-40 XP and the leftover rolls forward):

| Stat | Share | Ranks | Final |
|---|---|---|---|
| Heavy Weapons (specialised) | 30% | 145 | |
| Melee Defense | 15% | 112 | |
| Missile Weapons | 10% | 107 | |
| Healing | 5% | 97 | |
| Strength | 10% | 94 | 194 |
| Endurance | 10% | 93 | 193 |
| Coordination | 10% | 94 | 194 |
| Max Health | 5% | 91 | |
| Max Stamina | 5% | 90 | |

Spent 20,487,694, remainder 14,567 (planned from zero: live, the four trained skills start at 5 ranks / 526 XP each, so the live remainder is never compared). Quickness,
Focus and Self stay 10. Capacity is 150 x 194 = 29,100 (15,000 at creation). `IsMet(PlayerQualities)` gives `TemplateCheck` (`AllMet` is `template=ok`), as the mage's.

**The kit** (`FighterKit.Items`: a `GearItem` and a `FighterRole`; `FighterKitTests`). Total burden 4,860 (`FighterKit.Burden`, tested against the line sum).

| wcid | Item | Count | Burden each | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 331 | Mace (Heavy Weapons) | 1 | 675 | wielded (MeleeWeapon) |
| 45 / 47 / 81 / 56 / 115 | Leather Cap / Coat / Leggings / Gauntlets / Boots | 1 each | 100 / 810 / 960 / 270 / 420 | worn |
| 307 | Shortbow (Missile Weapons) | 1 | 450 | pack |
| 300 | Arrow | 200 (max 1000) | 5 | pack |
| 628 | Handy Healing Kit | 2 separate items | 50 | pack |
| 377 | Potion of Healing | 5 (max 100) | 15 | pack |

Kits do not stack, so two are granted. The bow and the arrows are not wielded by the kit (the combat slices wield them). `GearItem.Kind` is only the nearest of the mage
GearKit's kinds: use `FighterItem.Role`, and do not give `FighterKit` to the mage's `GearScenario`.

**`--scenario=fighter-build`** (`Session/Scenarios/FighterBuildScenario.cs`; `HeadlessScenario.FighterBuild`, wired in `Lifestone.Headless/Program.cs`, quit after 600 s;
`--character` and `--create-character` are ignored with a warning: the fighter is always the template's character). It finds `Holtburg Hunter` in the character list or creates it
with the template's creation intent, enters it alone, reads the status (Player access: the server mod must report `xp` and `item`), then builds, each step only if the read model
still needs it: (a) `xp` up to `XpForLevel(40)`; (b) `TrainSkill` for a template skill shown untrained (credits Melee Defense 10, Missile Weapons 6, Healing 6); (c) the raises of
`Targets` in plan order; (d) the kit, one `item` grant at a time (a stack line is topped up by the missing count, never merged; the healing kits count separately); (e) the Mace
wielded at its `ValidLocations` and the five leather pieces worn (the mage's wield pattern; a non-kit piece in the way goes to the pack first); (f) `PlayerTools.Place` to the
Holtburg point of interest when the character is not in its landblock (no bind: the hunt of 1.2 binds). Then it logs off and quits. The entered character's name is checked against
the template's before every building step (`NameCheck`): a null name waits, a different name fails and sends nothing.

The scenario is **idempotent**: a ready fighter sends nothing. The verdict is `fighter=ready|built|failed level=<n> template=ok|short [template_short=...] kit=ok|<Name:held/needed,...>
wielded=<wcid|0> worn=<n>` (logged once as `[fighter] verdict`, printed as `fighter-build <fields>`). The first live build gave `fighter=built level>=40 template=ok kit=ok wielded=331
worn=5`, and a second run `fighter=ready`.

Tests. `FighterBuildScenarioTests` (5, `Client.Tests/Session/Fighter/`) run the whole headless scenario on the scripted ACE (`ScriptedAceSession` with `ScriptedFighter`, a model of
ACE's `xp`, stat, `item`, wield and place answers; call `Pump()` each step): create from nothing, ready, partial, other characters untouched with a full account and a refused
grant, and the name check. The stand-in cannot run a build (no Login or EnterWorld), as for the mage. `FighterBuildLoopbackTests` (`Loopback.Tests/Session/`, the lead runs it)
asserts `fighter=ready level=40 template=ok`, a second run that sends nothing, and the Advancement chat (type 0x0D): "You are now level N!" after the xp step, "Your base ... is now N!"
per raise, none on the second run.

**`login-check`'s `fighter=`** (M4 1.1 s5). The verdict line gains ` fighter=<present|absent|pending-delete>` after `mage_char=` (`LoginCheckScenario.ReportFields`, `FighterOf`): whether
the account's character list (`GameState.Characters.Entries`) holds `Holtburg Hunter` (`FighterTemplate.CharacterName`), compared case-insensitively; `pending-delete` is listed but
marked for deletion, `n/a` is a run that never read the list. Report-only, like `level=` and `mage_char=` (section 27): it never fails the check.

**The protected name** (M4 D1). `Holtburg Hunter` is a graduated test character: `CharPurgeRules.Graduated` (the server mod) and `StandInCharPurge.Graduated` (the stand-in) hold it
after `BSD Mage`, a test pins the two lists equal, and `char purge` refuses it with `graduated`, in any case. It is levelled only by its build scenario and the loops' kills, and is
never reset or purged.

**M4 3.1 fix2: which item a step acts on (the lookup defect of the live gate).** The hunt's steps (the swap, the restore, the heals) choose the item by `FighterItems.Find` (`Session/Fighter/FighterItems.cs`): an EQUIPPED instance of the weenie class wins over a pack copy, otherwise a pack instance (the Mace the swap put aside is preferred for the restore). The held list (`GearInventory.Collect`) names the main pack and the side packs before the equipped items, and the hunt's loot adds duplicates of the kit's classes (a looted Shortbow, Mace, arrows), so the earlier first-match lookup read "not wielded" for the pack copy of a Shortbow ACE had just wielded and waited for ever (`still waiting after 10 s: a step the server confirmed is not in the read model yet: wield the Shortbow`); ACE then refused the Mace because a launcher was wielded (WeenieError 0, `CheckWeaponCollision`) and every later hunter test failed on it (the combat-mode guard of lane g refusing the melee mode with a Shortbow wielded, correctly). Each swap and restore step logs the object's guid. The restore reads the END STATE (the Mace wielded, no launcher, no ammunition) and repairs a half-done swap: wielded ammunition first, then the wielded launcher by its own guid, each waited for, then the Mace. `FighterBuildScenario` clears the weapon slots the same way (`ClearWeaponSlots`: the ammunition, then the launcher, into the pack) before it wields the Mace, so it is the repair tool for a hunter a half-done swap left holding the bow (it repaired the live hunter at the gate). `FighterItemsTests`, four `HuntScenarioTests` rows and two `FighterBuildScenarioTests` rows (names ending `_3_1_fix2`) pin it; `ScriptedFighter` refuses a melee wield while a launcher is wielded and the reverse, as ACE does. A melee hunt that STARTS with a launcher wielded still fails loudly at the guard; only its wind-down repairs the state.

**Open TODOs.**
- `TODO(m4-healing-minlevel)`: Healing has MinLevel 2 in the SkillTable; the live build created the character with Healing trained at level 1 and the template check passed, so ACE's
  creation (`PlayerFactory.Create`, ACE @95ef426d, reference only) accepted it. The name stays in the code, as the failure text, until a retail-side check closes it.
- `TODO(m4-specialize-later)`: a specialised skill that shows untrained cannot be trained later (a specialise costs credits `TrainSkill` does not spend); the build fails and names it.
  Not reached: Heavy Weapons is specialised at creation.
- `TODO(m4-kit-itemtype)` is closed in 3.1 s0: the Handy Healing Kit's and the Potion of Healing's ItemType is 0x80 (Misc), and the kit's BoostValue (PropertyInt 90) is 50 (the earlier "boost 16" was wrong): `FighterKit.ItemTypeMisc`.
- `TODO(m4-template-skill)` and `TODO(m4-template-attribute)`: a skill or attribute id the template has no name for throws.

## 35. The hunt loop and melee and missile (M4 1.2; D10, D12; DoD 3, 4)

M4's fights need the attack sends on the wire, a read of the attack state, the actions that wrap the sends, a stand-in
that answers them offline, and a scenario that hunts the Holtburg belt with the melee weapon or the bow. The code is the
reference; this section says where things are.

**The wire** (`src/Lifestone.Net`). Three client sends: GameAction 0x0008 TargetedMeleeAttack {target, height, power},
0x000A TargetedMissileAttack {target, height, accuracy} (both start a run) and 0x01B7 CancelAttack (ends it). The codecs
are `Messages/Actions/CombatActionCodecs.cs`, a port of OpenAC's `AttackTargetRequest.cs` at the f22b048 pin
(section 9.1 of [protocol.md](protocol.md); [third-party/net.md](third-party/net.md), "The M4 combat wire"). `IAcSession`
sends them in the world as `SendMeleeAttack`, `SendMissileAttack` and `SendCancelAttack` (`SessionMachine.World.cs`). A
NaN or infinite power or accuracy throws ArgumentException before any sequence is used. ACE clamps the values to 0..1
itself (`W/Player_Melee.cs` 90, `W/Player_Missile.cs` 82); OpenAC does not, so the codecs send the value given. The
inbound events are GameEvent 0x01A7 AttackDone {WeenieError} and 0x01B8 CombatCommenceAttack (no payload), in
`Messages/Combat/AttackEventCodecs.cs`, dispatched through `IInboundMessageHandler.OnAttackDone` and `OnCommenceAttack`,
which the reducer overrides (`Session/State/GameStateReducer.Combat.cs`). Retail's senders are
`CM_Combat::Event_TargetedMeleeAttack` (11.6096 0x006AAB70, 11.4186 0x006A9C10), `Event_TargetedMissileAttack`
(0x006AACC0, 0x006A9D60) and `Event_CancelAttack` (0x006AA940, 0x006A99E0). The retail handlers of 0x01A7 and 0x01B8 are
not cited yet: `TODO(m4-attack-done-retail-cite)`. The stand-in side is in section 16.

**The attack state** (`Session/Contracts/AttackSequence.cs`; `IGameSessionView.Attack`, `IGameSessionView.Ammo`).
One send gives a whole run: with AutoRepeatAttacks on for the character, ACE walks the player to the target and
repeats the swing (or the shot) while the target lives, and the client sends nothing between swings
(`cache/m4/1-2/capture.md` C1 and C3). `AttackSequence` is the reducer's record of that run. Its `State` is `Idle`;
`Started` on the player's own attack-motion F74C, an arrow's launch or 0x01B8; `SwingOver` on 01A7 {0}, one swing over
with the next pending; and `Done` on 01A7 with a non-zero code (0x0036 at a kill, a cancel, a refusal, or a target gone).
It also holds `Target`, `TargetName`, `Swings` (own attack-motion F74C events, melee), `Shots` (falls of the wielded
ammunition's 0x0197 stack, missile), `LastWeenieError` and `Number` (the run's count). A TurnToObject F74C that follows a
send is not a swing. The reducer publishes `AttackChanged` on every 01A7, changed or not, so a tracker sees a refusal
that follows a Done at once (`TODO(m4-attack-done-event)`, closed in s4b). `Ammo` is `HeldAmmo` (wcid, guid, stack), null
when no ammunition is wielded. `NearestCreature` (`Session/Contracts/WorldObjectTable.cs`) finds the nearest creature
by a name list and a range. The creature's level is not on the wire, so a creature of unknown level is skipped, never
guessed in (`TODO(m4-creature-level)`); the hunt supplies the level from its ground's table by wcid.

**The actions and the trackers** (`Session/Actions/ActionApi.cs`, `Session/Actions/Trackers/CombatTrackers.cs`;
`AttackTrackerBase`, `MeleeAttackTracker`, `MissileAttackTracker`, `CancelAttackTracker`). `IActionApi` has `Attack`,
`MissileAttack` and `CancelAttack`. They run in lane `ActionLane.Attack`: one in flight at a time, in call order, no
rate limit. Timeouts run from the send: `ActionPolicy.AttackSeconds` 20 s (`UseSeconds`), `MissileAttackSeconds` 10 s,
`CancelAttackSeconds` 5 s. The completion rules:

- *Melee.* Confirmed at the first sign ACE took the send: the player's own attack-motion F74C, a TurnToObject F74C naming
  the target (the approach's MoveToObject is also an F74C, 4 ms after the send; a refusal sends no motion), or 01B8. The
  run's end is the reducer's, not the tracker's.
- *Missile.* Confirmed at the first sign of the launch: the run's start, the wielded ammunition's 0x0197 stack falling, or
  the arrow's CreateObject F745 (taken for ours by the war-spell projectile rule). The last arrow may take the stack with
  no 0x0197, so its F745 is the sign (`TODO(m4-last-arrow-launch)`: `AttackSequence.Shots` does not count it).
- *Queued.* An attack sent while a run is under way gets no answer from ACE (it queues the power, `E/AttackQueue.cs`), so
  it is Confirmed at once.
- *Refusals.* A refusal is a WeenieError (0x028A, or 0x028B with a string) or the transient 02EB, then 01A7. Melee is
  refused by WeenieError 0x04EB or 0x04CC, by a too-busy code, and by a 02EB that says "cannot attack". A missile is
  refused by the transient 02EB texts only (R9, 1.3: "You cannot attack X" and "You are out of ammunition!"), and by 0x0550 (out of range), 0x03F9, 0x001D, 0x04EB or 0x04CC. The tracker settles the refusal at
  the end of the tick in which the error was seen, so the 01A7 need not be observable. These WeenieError names come from
  the spec, not from a read of ACE's enum in this slice. A silent refusal (a 01A7 with a code and no start) is
  `ServerError` with the code kept: there is no Failed kind.
- *Cancel.* Confirmed on the run's Done; `Sent` at once when none runs.

**The stand-in** (`Session/StandIn/StandInWorld.Attack.cs`; section 16). It answers the three sends in capture.md's
order. Its placeholders all log once per run as `TODO(m4-standin-...)`: `attack-timing`, `approach-walk`,
`moveto-params`, `swing-pick`, `player-damage`, `creature-corpse`, `aim-motions`, `weapon-rows`, `weapon-kinds`,
`launch-encumbrance`, `arrow-model`. The launcher has no weapon row, so its range uses ACE's default launcher velocity of
20 m/s: 40.8 m. Unmodelled, with no name yet: the 02CF and 02DD skill-use pair in the hit tick, and the 02EB stance switch
of a bow wielded with no ammunition. `StandInMeleeTests` (8) and `StandInMissileTests` (6) cover the sends.

**The heal stand-in and the recovery rule (M4 3.1 s2, s3).** With `StandInVisit.Heals` (or `HealItems`) the hunt's potions drink for real in the stand-in: 25 health, the stack message and 1.47 s of busy (the hunt's own tests keep the old fixture, a potion that heals nothing, by leaving the flag off; the recovery tests set it). `StandInWorld.Heal.cs` answers the kit and the potion as section 16 says. With no potion to drink, a kit whose estimated chance is under `--heal-chance` is no longer waited out: when its chance is at least `--heal-floor-chance` (default 0.3, the constant `RecoveryRules.KitFloorChance`) and fewer than two rolls were lost at this health, the kit is used as the last resort before the regeneration wait (`RecoveryDecision.KitLastResort`; the log line says "last resort"). Under the floor the rule is as before: wait up to 300 s, fail at once under the 30% retreat share.

**The recovery rule (M4 3.1 s3; `Session/Fighter/RecoveryRules.cs`, `RecoveryOptions.cs`; closes `TODO(m4-hunt-recovery)`).** Pure and allocation-free per decision. `HealChance.Estimate(healing, boost, specialised, missing, combat)` is ACE's formula (`Healer.DoSkillCheck`): eff = round((Healing + BoostValue) x 1.5 specialised else 1.1), diff = round(missing x 2 x 1.1 in a combat mode, x 1.0 otherwise), chance = 1/(1+e^(-0.03 (eff - diff))); pinned against the live capture (eff 264: 145 missing in combat 0.16, 72 missing 0.96). A recovery starts under `--heal-below` (default 75% of the maximum health) and, once started, goes on to `--heal-until` (90%). At each look: the **kit** while it has uses, fewer than two rolls were lost in a row at this health, and its estimated chance is at least `--heal-chance` (0.7: at 188 health that is 81 or more health in combat, 71 or more in peace); else a **potion** (25 points, no roll) while the missing health is at least 15 (a potion at a full vital is spent for nothing, capture C3); else, with the share at or over the trigger, nothing; under the retreat share (30%) a loud failure at once; else **wait** for regeneration, then a loud failure after 300 s. A lost roll does not change the health, so two in a row set the kit aside for that health (the driver clears the count when the health moves). The Healing skill is read from the read model (`RecoveryRules.ReadHealingSkill`: skill 21, (Focus + Coordination) / 3 plus init and ranks through `SkillRules.TryCurrent`; the formula is pinned by `RecoveryRulesDatTests`), every 5 s; an untrained skill sets the kit aside (ACE's 0x04FC), an unreadable one falls back to 190 (the capture's value) loudly once (`TODO(m4-heal-skill-read)`: the stand-in's description lists no skill, so offline it always falls back). A kit or potion refused twice in a row (Healing untrained at once) is set aside for the run. More than 40 heals in one recovery fails the run (a safety net, never a rule). `--heal on|off` (default on; off runs no recovery at all, the retreat still protects), `--heal-below PCT`, `--heal-until PCT` (not under below), `--heal-chance P` (0 to 1), parsed in `HuntOptions.FromArguments` (a bad value is an error), registered in `OptionCatalog.Hunt.cs` for the main scene and the headless host. **Not built:** a heal during a fight; the retreat at 30% stays the in-fight rule (`TODO(m4-hunt-infight-heal)`, logged once in the run's first lines). Tests: `RecoveryRulesTests` (the decisions at their boundaries, the capture's numbers, two lost rolls, the wait and the failure, the options), `HuntScenarioTests` over the stand-in's heal, `HuntOptionsTests`.

**The hunt ground** (`Session/Fighter/HuntGround.cs`, `HuntGroundDatTests`). A `HuntGroundRow` is data: name, level band,
creature names, cells and `InUse`. `HuntGround.LowB` is the Low B belt, band 8 to 20. Its names are those of the
level-8 row (Banderling Scout, Blue Phyntos Wasp, Outcast Monouga, Field Ursuin, Small Shadow Child, Red Phyntos Drone),
the level-15 row and the level-20 row. Its cells are the ten south-west cells nearest Holtburg, in the world database's
order, the first `0xA5B00032`. A cell's centre is `(x*24+12, y*24+12)` with `x=(i-1)/8` and `y=(i-1)%8`; `PlacePoint`
puts the player at the terrain height there (`HuntGround.TerrainHeight`) plus 0.5 m. `HuntGroundDatTests` closes
`TODO(m4-belt-cell-terrain)`: water is a corner vertex of terrain type 16 to 20 (ACE's table), and slope is the lowest
surface normal over a 5 x 5 grid against `PhysicsGlobals.FloorZ` (0.664). The finding: all ten cells are dry and flat
(lowest normal z 0.993). What stands on a cell is not read (`TODO(m4-belt-cell-objects)`). `HuntGround.LowNorthForestMix` is the second ground (M4 3.1 s0; `--hunt-ground=belt|forest`, default belt): band 30 to 40 (the hunter is level 40; spec 5.9's 30 to 60 narrowed), `LowNorthForestTable` of 38 creatures by wcid with level (the lead's SELECT of generator 21183; the NPC Fisana the Jeweler and the Tusker Shrine Statue are left out), and 33 cells, the 0x9FB* strip 7.7 to 8.6 map units west, nearest first, first `0x9FB50039`; `HuntGroundDatTests` finds all 33 dry and flat (lowest normal z 0.973 or more) and pins the ten nearest as Dry, which closes `TODO(m4-forest-cells)`. A scenario that names a row whose `InUse` is false still fails with `TODO(m4-hunt-ground-unused)`.

**The hunt scenario** (`Session/Scenarios/HuntScenario.cs`; `--scenario=hunt`, `HeadlessScenario.Hunt`). It acts only
through `IActionApi` and `PlayerTools`. Its stages, in order:

1. *Name.* The entered character must be Holtburg Hunter; `fighter=wrong` fails before anything is sent.
2. *Bind.* `place poi` to Holtburg when the character is not in its landblock, a 3 s settle, the nearest lifestone (an
   object with the lifestone description flag), then `Use`. Once. A failed bind is logged and the run goes on unbound
   (`TODO(m4-hunt-bind)`).
3. *Health* (the driver of the recovery rule, M4 3.1 s3). Before the first fight (from the bind) and between kills (after the corpse sequence ends and the attack's Done is in, before the next acquire; never during a fight), `HealthStep` asks `RecoveryRules.Decide` and acts: the kit (`UseWithTarget(kit, self)`), a potion (`Drink`), `heal-wait` (regeneration, up to 300 s), a loud failure, or nothing (the stage returns to `swap` or `ground` at the first look, to `acquire` between kills). Each decision logs one line (`heal: kit 0x... (N use(s) left) at 43% (108 missing), estimated chance 0.69 ...`, `heal: potion ...: the kit's chance 0.31 is under 0.7`), and each outcome one line with the amount (`heal: the kit restored 83 Health (19 left)`, `heal: the kit's roll was lost ...`). The old floor (`StartFloor`, `MaxPotions`, `PotionRiseSeconds`) is gone. The Heal lane (s1) holds the busy chain, so the driver waits for each handle's Outcome and adds no wait of its own, with one exception: a heal answered 0x001D (the server's busy: a pick-up or the corpse's close just before is another lane's) is sent again after 1 s, logged, at most 10 times per recovery (`HealBusyRetrySeconds`, `MaxHealBusyRetries`); the stand-in showed it right after a loot. 
4. *Swap* (missile only), in capture.md C2's order: NonCombat; the Mace to the pack (0x0019); the Shortbow wielded (0x001A,
   refused while the Mace is wielded: GE 00A0 with WeenieError None, `Player_Inventory.cs` 1663-1668); then the arrows
   wielded (0x800000). Missile mode comes after the arrows: a bow wielded in a combat mode with no ammunition brings a
   02EB "out of ammunition" stance switch (`Player_Combat.cs` 843-856).
5. *Ground.* `place loc` to the first usable cell.
6. *Acquire.* `NearestCreature` within 60 m (35 m under the bow) of the creatures whose weenie class id is in the
   ground's table (`HuntGround.LowBTable`: 37 creatures of generators 1999 and 2001 with their levels, from a read-only
   SELECT on release v0.9.296) and whose table level is in the ground's band, 8 to 20. The level is not on the wire
   (`TODO(m4-creature-level)`), so it is the table's, keyed by wcid; the level-30 Tumerok Warrior is listed and the band
   filters it. A creature whose wcid is not in the table is never a target: there is no fallback
   (`TODO(m4-hunt-unlisted-creature)` is closed). None in range: the next cell, at most four cells in all, then the run
   fails. A refused target is excluded apart from its level.
7. *Query* (`QueryHealth`), then *mode* (SetCombatMode Melee or Missile).
8. *Attack.* One `Attack` or `MissileAttack` (Medium height, power or accuracy 0.5) per run. ACE walks the player in and
   repeats the run; the next send waits for the run's Done and a living target. A refusal (any non-Confirmed outcome,
   the 02EB "You cannot attack" included) excludes the target. A kill waits for the Done or 3 s. A melee
   approach is cancelled by ACE once the player is 15 m from where it began (`FailDistance`, `Player_Move.cs` 167 and
   241; 028A `YouChargedTooFar` 0x003D, then 01A7 0x0036 with no swing). The hunt reads that 01A7 at once (an
   `AttackChanged` whose number is still the send's) and retries the Attack from where the player stopped, as long as each
   retry closed at least 3 m, at most 6 (`charge_retries=`): the walk to a far target is ACE's own approach in 15 m legs,
   not a client walker (`MeleeAttackTracker` stays Confirmed: the approach did start). Any other code before a swing
   excludes the target. A sequence still
   Active at the next send waits at most 13 s (`KillDoneSeconds` + `WaitSeconds`); then the hunt logs it, sends
   `CancelAttack` and excludes the target (`stalls=` in the detail line). The reducer resets the attack sequence
   when the world is left (log off, enter world).

Every ending winds down the same way: CancelAttack if a run is under way; NonCombat; the weapon restored (the arrows and
the bow to the pack, the Mace wielded, so that the fighter-build scenario still reads `wielded=331`); `place poi` to
Holtburg; log off; quit. A health fall under 30% or the `--hunt-seconds` limit ends the fight through this wind-down and
fails the run, with `returned=yes`. A run that cannot get back reports `returned=no`. The `endSession` flag (default
true) makes the run end at the return for a host that logs off itself: the offline stand-in refuses the log off and the
quit.

*The fields.* `HuntTally` (`Session/Scenarios/HuntTally.cs`; not `FightTally`, which reads a mage's damage from chat) gives
`fighter=ok|wrong kills swings shots hits misses damage_dealt damage_taken hits_taken evades xp_gained returned=yes|no`
(`VerdictFields()`). A blow is the combat notice 0x01B1 (hits, damage_dealt); a dodge 0x01B3 and the "hit the environment"
lines are misses; the target's blows 0x01B2 and 0x01B4 are hits_taken, damage_taken and evades; swings and shots are the
runs' counts summed; kills and xp are `FightTally`'s. `damage_taken` also sums a creature's spell lines (the Magic-channel
"X bashes you for 32 points with Shock Wave II.", as `FightTally` does); `hits_taken` counts the 0x01B2 notices only (the
live melee run of 2026-10-20: 32 points of 01B2 plus a 32-point spell line made the 64 the health lost, and the share's
maximum read 188 as it should). `xp_gained` is the total-experience delta, not a kill count: the same run gained 487 with
no kill, in three steps on a first evade, a first landed hit and a spell resist (ACE's skill-use experience; the exact rule
is not read). All are report-only in 1.2. `DetailFields()` adds `weapon bound potions cells attacks refused restored stalls
health_start_pct health_end_pct` (the last two report-only: the health share read at the first Step of the health stage and
at the last Step).

*Options* (`Session/Scenarios/HuntOptions.cs`, `OptionCatalog.Hunt.cs`): `--hunt-kills` (1 to 1000, default 1),
`--hunt-seconds` (default 300) and `--hunt-weapon=melee|missile` (default melee) and `--hunt-ground=belt|forest` (default belt; the `name=value` form only) chooses the ground row: `HuntOptions.GroundRow` is passed to `HuntScenario` by the headless host and by `HuntLegRunner`, whose worker reads the terrain heights of the chosen row's usable cells; the scenario's first hunt line names the ground (`ground Low North Forest Mix: 33 usable cell(s) of 33`), and the target rule is the belt's, with the ground's table and band. The default quit-after is 600 s plus
`--hunt-seconds`. The account rule is the other character scenarios' (`test` only). The summary line `hunt <fields>` is
printed before `headless done`.

*Tests.* `HuntScenarioTests` (`tests/Lifestone.Client.Tests/Session/Fighter/`, 10) run the scenario on the offline
stand-in: melee kill and return, missile with the swap and the restore, a refused target excluded, the next cell, the
unlisted fallback, nothing near, potions and the regeneration wait, the retreat under 30%, the wrong character, the forest
row refused, and the options.

**The leg: `--login-check=hunt`** (`src/Lifestone.Game/scripts/Session/HuntLegRunner.cs`; `LoginCheckKind.Hunt`, hooked
by `HuntLegRunner.Install` in `LoginCheckRunner.Install`). It enters Holtburg Hunter on the test account (absent: it fails
and says to run `--scenario=fighter-build`), settles for 3 s, runs `HuntScenario(endSession: false)`, logs off and quits.
The options pass through; a bad value fails the leg at its first frame. The terrain heights of the ten belt cells are
read on a worker before the hunt. Limits: login 60 s, prepare (bind, belt, swap) 300 s, `--hunt-seconds` (default 300),
wind-down 120 s: 780 s without the shop visits, and 1,200 s with `--sell on` or `--buy-kits` above 0 (`HuntScenario.VendorTripsSeconds`, 420 s added); a recovery log off of up to 120 s more. Run it with `-TimeoutSeconds 1200`, and smoke's `login-hunt` Godot timeout is 1,500 s.

The verdict line is `[logincheck] hunt done ok=<true|false> kills damage_dealt damage_taken hits_taken evades xp_gained
fighter swings shots hits misses returned=<yes|no> attack_frame_max_ms=<x|n/a>` (M3's six in M3's order, then the five new, then 1.3's). `HuntLegVerdict`
(`Session/Scenarios/HuntLegVerdict.cs`) judges `ok` on three things: no failure (the run reached done, wind-down
included), no death notice, `kills>=1` with `damage_dealt>0`, and from 1.3 `swings>=1` (melee) or `shots>=1` with `hits>=1` (missile). The rest are report-only, `attack_frame_max_ms` among them (judged from 2.1). A `hunt detail` line
follows with the seconds, the options, the scenario's `DetailFields` and the frame note.

**The shops (M4 3.2 s4a).** Back in Holtburg (`Home`, after `wind-restore` and `wind-home`, so the Mace is wielded and the bow is in the pack) the hunt visits Renald the Elder with `--sell on` (default) and Thelnoth Cort with `--buy-kits N` (default 2, 0 off) before it logs off: `place loc` into the shop at the vendor's own position (cells 0xA9B40141 and 0xA9B40111; `TODO(m4-vendor-approach)` names the walk through the door that is not built), the vendor looked up by weenie class (718, 715), `OpenVendor`, then `Sell` in batches of 20 (`SellRules`) or `Buy` of the missing Handy Healing Kits (the 0062 row for wcid 628, cut to what the pyreals cover), then `place poi` Holtburg again. A failure there never fails the hunt: `sell=failed:<reason>` or `buy=failed:<reason>` on the detail line. No visit is made after a failed hunt or when nothing is to be sold or bought (`sell=none`, `buy=enough`). The leg line carries `sold=<n> bought=<n> pyreals=<delta> appraised=<n> vendor_frame_max_ms=<x|n/a>` beside `mode_mismatch=<n>` (all report-only in 3.2; `sold>=1` and `vendor_frame_max_ms` are judged at 4.2), and the scenario's detail line ends `sold= bought= pyreals= appraised= sell= buy= buy_pyreals=`. `pyreals` is the sale's coin delta only; the purchase's price is `buy_pyreals`. `--appraise-loot` (default on) identifies each item the corpse loop picks up once (the scenario diffs the held guids against a snapshot taken when the loop began; `CorpseLooter` is unchanged). The vendor review frame is `<stem>-vendor.png`, 1 s after Renald's 0062, either weapon, taken whatever shows; its log line says `vendor panel visible: <class name>|no`.

*Review frames* (`--play-screenshot=PATH`, no `--play-screenshot-after`; 800x600, the developer HUD hidden). The frame
is the one the weapon gives (`HuntShotPlan`). Melee: `<stem>-swing.png`, 0.3 s after the first own swing of a run (the
run's Swings reaches 1), with the target within 6 m, the distance re-checked at the frame's moment. Missile:
`<stem>-arrow.png`, 0.1 s after the first arrow's F745 (wcid 300, in the world), only if the arrow still exists then. A
failed condition drops the trigger and waits for the next one; the scenario is held at most 3 s. The log names the frame,
its trigger, the target and its distance, the wielded weapon's wcid and the HUD state; the end note names a frame whose
trigger never came. `HuntLegVerdictTests` (11) and `HuntLegRunnerTests` (6) cover the verdict and the leg.

**The live tests** (the lead runs them; `tests/Lifestone.Loopback.Tests/Session/`): `MeleeLiveTests`, `MissileLiveTests` and
`HuntLiveTests` (with `HuntLiveRun`, the shared harness). Their recordings go to `cache/m4/1-2/live/`. `AttackCaptureLiveTests`
was the research capture and is removed; `cache/m4/1-2/capture.md` is its note.

**The smoke step `login-hunt`** (`tools/smoke.ps1`): `--account=test --login-check=hunt --hunt-weapon=melee --hunt-kills=3`,
with the Godot run given 1,500 s (1,200 s until 3.2 added the shop visits). Since M4 3.1 s4 the step runs `--hunt-kills=3`, so `looted>=1` is judged over two or more kills (`HuntLegVerdict.LootJudgeMinKills`); the leg stops itself at `--hunt-seconds` (default 300) of hunting whatever the count, so the 1,500 s covers three kills with the recoveries between them (checked against the leg's own limits, not by a run). It runs only under `-Backend require`; under `auto` and `start` it is SKIP, with the
message `login-bsd` uses. Its judge is the leg's `hunt done ok=true` line (from 1.3: `kills>=1`, `damage_dealt>0`, and `swings>=1` for melee or `shots>=1` with `hits>=1` for missile). `swings`,
`shots`, `hits`, `misses`, `returned` and `fighter` are copied into the summary report-only (`n/a` when the line lacks
one). `frame_over_50` is read as for the other judged live legs (worst of three, the contended rule of D42).
`attack_frame_max_ms` is copied into the summary report-only (`n/a` when the line lacks it) and is not judged until 2.1 (D11). Since 3.1 s4 the summary copies, report-only, `heals kit_uses potions mode_mismatch` from the verdict line and `heal_failed_rolls heal_points heal_wasted` from the `[logincheck] hunt detail` line. The step is in `$backendSteps` and in
`$frameJudgedSteps` after `login-death`; `slice-check` never runs it.

**The live order** (`cache/m4/1-2/capture.md`, 2026-10-20, ACE 95ef426d, Holtburg Hunter at belt cell `0xA5B00032`). With
AutoRepeatAttacks on, one 0x0008 gave five swings and one 0x000A gave five arrows. Each repeat opens with 01B8. A 01C0
comes in the same tick as every 01B1 and 01AD; the spec (`docs/milestones/m4-fights-and-loot.md` 5.3) says no 01C0 follows
the player's own hit, and the capture shows one. A kill is 01AD with no 01B1, then the corpse, then 01A7 {0x0036}, which
can trail a teleport by about a second. The bow wield is refused (GE 00A0) while a melee weapon is wielded, so the swap
goes in the order above.

**Live round 3 fixes (fix3).**
- *The client walks ACE's approach itself (R1).* ACE's attack answer is the player's MoveToObject F74C and no F748 for the player; the client's physics carries the MoveTo out and reports its autonomous
  positions (retail `CPhysics::SetObjectMovement`, 11.4186 0x00509690, 11.6096 0x0050A160, runs the server's MoveTo in the physics object's update; the report cadence is the controller's, otherwise unknown).
  Round 3's headless hunt had no physics player, so its position never moved and the retry rule excluded targets 28-59 m away. `HuntFollow` (Scenarios/HuntFollow.cs) is the headless hunt's body: an
  `OfflineWalker` as `RoutePlay` and `DenWalk` make it, in a physics world that attaches the collision of each landblock the server puts the player in (and the eight around it) on first need, placed by the server's
  teleports and given the server's positions and MoveTos. `HuntScenario` ticks it each frame and measures every approach distance from it (the log says `own body`), at each send and in the retry line; the retry
  rule is unchanged (at most 6, each at least 3 m closer). Game: the player controller's walker is already the body (`AvatarSet.SetMovement` gives the self MoveTo to `PhysicsPlayerBody.ApplyServerMovement`),
  and `HuntLegRunner.PlayerBodyFollow` only reports its position to the hunt. Offline: `StandInWorld.ApproachSendsPositions = false` (and `ClientPosition`) models "MoveToObject, no F748"; the test sink hands the body's
  positions to the stand-in. `TODO(m4-hunt-follow-collision)` and `TODO(m4-hunt-follow-unplaced)` are the loud paths.
- *Two Arrow stacks (R2).* The swap merges every Arrow stack into the largest (`MergeStack`, waited for), wields that stack by its guid and checks it by that guid; the restore puts the wielded stack back. The restore
  now reads the end state (the Mace wielded, no bow, no arrows) whatever the run did, and `restored=` comes from that state, not from whether the swap ran.

**Open TODOs.**
- `TODO(m4-attack-done-retail-cite)`: the retail handlers of 0x01A7 and 0x01B8 (the transplant table has the senders only).
- `TODO(m4-creature-level)`: the creature's level is not in CreateObject; the hunt reads it from the ground's table by wcid (the belt's `LowBTable` and the forest's `LowNorthForestTable`: both are read, the forest half closed by 3.1 s0).
- `TODO(m4-last-arrow-launch)`: the last arrow may delete its stack with no 0x0197, so it is not counted in `Shots`.
- `TODO(m4-belt-cell-objects)`: the buildings and scenery on a belt cell are not read.
- `TODO(m4-forest-cells)` is closed in 3.1 s0 (the forest row has 33 cells, all dry and flat).
- `TODO(m4-hunt-bind)`: the scenario's bind (a failed bind is logged and the run goes on unbound).
- `TODO(m4-hunt-ground-unused)`: for a row whose `InUse` is false (no row is unused now; a copy of the forest row with `InUse` false tests the path).
- `TODO(m4-hunt-recovery)` is closed in 3.1 s3 (the recovery rule, above).
- The stand-in placeholders named in the stand-in paragraph above (`TODO(m4-standin-...)`).

Closed in 1.2: `m4-hunt-unlisted-creature` (no fallback: an unlisted creature is never a target), `m4-standin-attack` (the stand-in answers the sends), `m4-attack-wire-ctor` (the reducer's constructor
calls `WireAttack`), `m4-attack-done-event` (every 01A7 is published), `m4-belt-cell-terrain`, and the two stand-in limits
of s4a (`m4-standin-swing-motion`, `m4-standin-approach`), replaced by `m4-standin-swing-pick` and `m4-standin-approach-walk`.

**M4 1.3: targeting and the combat UI (D11; DoD 3 and 4).** `AttackRequested` (§5) is the event of each send that reaches the wire. `attack_frame_max_ms` is the worst frame from an attack's send to its run's Done, over every run of the leg (`AttackFrameSampler`). It is on the hunt line, on the attack play-check rows (`PlayChecks.Attack.cs`) and in smoke's summary. It is report-only in 1.3 and judged from 2.1 (D11). The leg's judge is as the leg paragraph above says: melee `kills>=1 damage_dealt>0 swings>=1`, missile `shots>=1 hits>=1`. The melee review frames are four. `swing` is 0.3 s after the first own swing (the 1.2 frame, `HuntShotPlan`). `c1-combat` is the combat window in melee. `c1-powerbar` is the combat window's meter mid-charge, from half the run's power to 0.05 short of it (`HuntUiShotPlan.MidCharge`; review 1.3-R2: at the first level above 0 the frame read as empty). `meter` is the toolbar's health meter of the selected target, lower than at its selection after a swing. `HuntUiShotPlan` (`Session/Scenarios/HuntUiShotPlan.cs`) holds the last three and the framing. Each needs the target within 6 m and an unblocked camera, and is held as the 1.2 frame is. The review hunt's chase camera is framed for the run (`m4-hunt-frame-camera`, closed in code; the lead's live frames are its proof). Missile keeps `arrow`. The live frames and the live hunt are the lead's to run. `TODO(m4-last-arrow-launch)` is logged once per run when the last arrow's F745 is taken as the launch with no 0x0197 (`MissileAttackTracker`). **M4 3.1 p: the projection rule runs live.** The live hunt frames of 1.3 and 2.2 printed `player_box=n/a`, because `WorldPicking.TryProjectIncludingPlayer` required the server objects layer's record of a draw (`ServerObjectsLayer.IsDrawn`), which the layer never makes for the player (its `_Process` draws the avatars that are not `IsPlayer`; the player view draws the player). `SelectionProjectionRules.Refusal` (Lifestone.Client/Play) keeps the three tests (body drawn, not held, drawn by the layer) and exempts the player from the layer test; the thresholds (30% overlap, the 35-degree side turn, the combat window's top) and `HuntUiShotPlan.TargetVisible` are unchanged. If a player box is missing again the first review frame logs `hunt: the player's box did not project at the first review frame` with a `why=` naming the input (`WorldPicking.WhyNotProjected`). **The heal and potion frames (M4 3.1 p):** slots 4 and 5 of `HuntUiShotPlan` (`<stem>-heal.png`, `<stem>-potion.png`), taken 1 s after a kit use whose roll won (`HealTally.Heals` rose) and after a drink that restored points (`Potions` rose, `Wasted` did not, `Points` rose), each triggered once and framed as `c1-combat` is (the framed camera; no target is asked for, the hunt heals between kills), held at most 3 s; a trigger that fails at its moment is dropped and waits for the next heal; the leg's end note (`hunt: retail-UI review frames: ...`) names a frame whose trigger never came. The runner reads `HuntScenario.HealTally`, never the scenario's private state.

m4-plan.md section 9 rows R8 and R9 are closed. R8: the melee and missile rows judge the session (`IGameSessionView.Attack` State Done, Swings and Shots, `LastWeenieError` 0x36, the 01AD kill notice, the target gone, and the `Ammo` stack falling by the shots); the stand-in's counters are diagnostic text. R9: `MissileAttackTracker` refuses on "You cannot attack X" and "You are out of ammunition!" only. Any other 02EB during a missile attack is logged once as `TODO(m4-missile-transient-text)` and does not refuse; this supersedes the "any 02EB" refusal in the wire paragraph above.

**M4 2.1: the hunt opens its kill's corpse (s4a; `--hunt-open-corpse`, default on; off restores 1.2).**
`HuntScenario.EnterKillWait` snapshots the corpses already in the object table and the target's last position. `KillWait`
then runs `StepCorpse` beside the wait for the attack's last 01A7 {0x0036}: Seek (the first F745 named "Corpse of <target
name>" within `CorpseRangeMetres` (6 m) of the target's last position, not in the snapshot, waited for at most
`CorpseAppearSeconds` (5 s) from the kill notice; capture C2 saw 0.54 s) -> `IActionApi.OpenContainer` (in the combat
stance, which ACE accepted) -> Confirmed: the viewed container's entries are logged (name and wcid each) -> once the attack
is over, `CloseContainer`. There is no loot here (2.2's). A corpse that does not appear is counted (`CorpsesMissed`, a
warning); an open that is not Confirmed (a ServerError, TimedOut or Refused) is counted (`CorpsesRefused`); neither fails
the hunt. The retreat rule (health under 30%) and the hunt's seconds abandon the sequence at once (an open view is closed
fire-and-forget), so the sequence never delays the retreat. The detail line gains `corpses_missed=` and `corpses_refused=`.
Verdict fields, appended after `attack_frame_max_ms`: `corpses_opened=<n>` (opens Confirmed) and `corpse_items=<n>` (entries
seen, summed). They are report-only; the lead judges `corpses_opened>=1` at the item's gate by reading the line.
`attack_frame_max_ms` is judged from 2.1 (D11): over 75 ms fails the leg (`ok=false`, the problem
`attack_frame_max_ms=<x> (at most 75 expected)`), and over 50 ms ends the line with ` frame_over_50=yes`, the same flag as
the bsd-fight, buff and death legs. The window is `AttackFrameSampler`'s (an attack's send to the 01A7 that ends its run);
an unmeasured value (n/a) is not judged. The line is now `... returned= attack_frame_max_ms= corpses_opened= corpse_items=[
frame_over_50=yes]`.

**M4 2.2: the hunt loots its kill (s0; `--loot`, default on; off restores 2.1).** After `ListCorpse` the hunt enters `CorpsePhase.Looting` and runs a `CorpseLooter` (`Session/Fighter/CorpseLooter.cs`) over the viewed corpse: one `IActionApi.PickUp(item, player, 0, ActionSource.Host)` at a time in `ContainerView.Entries` order, a new one only when the previous handle has an Outcome (the Inventory lane holds the busy chain; the loop adds no wait; the capture of 2026-10-21: about 2.8 s an item from a corpse). The rule is `LootRules` (below). The corpse is closed once the loot is Done and the attack's last 01A7 is in; the retreat rule and the hunt's seconds still abandon the sequence (the open view is closed fire-and-forget, and the looter says `stopped_by=abandoned`). A monster corpse rots after 300 s, so the loot has no other limit. The tally counts `looted` (PickUps Confirmed) and `loot_skipped` (entries the rule left, for every reason); `DetailFields` prints both, and the leg prints `looted=` and `loot_skipped=` after `corpse_items=`.

**The loot rule** (`LootRules`, DoD 8): take an item when its description's `Value` is at least 1 (`--loot-min-value`) and at most 1,000,000, its `ItemType` has a bit of the vendor mask (`--loot-types`, default `shop` = 0x480467A7, Renald the Elder 718; `all`; or a hex mask), and the burden stays at or under 100% of the capacity (`--loot-max-burden`, `EncumbranceSystem.Load` as the inventory page computes it, the item's own burden from the weenie description). The checks run value, type, burden; the skip reasons are `not_resolved already_held value type burden`. The mask is the shard's weenie 718 (the lead's SELECT), not a wire value: `TODO(m4-loot-rules-shop-mask)` (3.2's 0062 carries it live). The vendor's accept rule is ACE's `W/Vendor.cs`; retail's client-side address is unknown.

**The loop's rules** (`CorpseLooter`): an unresolved entry (its F745 not in) is left and judged when it arrives, for 3 s from the first step, then counted `not_resolved`; a ServerError or Refused move is counted `Refused` and the loop goes on, except WeenieError 0x002A / "You are too encumbered to carry that!" (028A, or the 02EB text), which ends it (`stopped_by=burden`); a 25 s timeout is counted `TimedOut`. There is no merge case: ACE never merges at pick-up (the capture of 2026-10-21: arrows and coins), and a looted stack is its own stack with a 0022. `TODO(m4-loot-merge-stacks)`: a looted stackable with a held stack of its class stays a separate stack; the hunt logs it once per run and does not call `MergeStack` in 2.2.

**`loot_frame_max_ms`** (`LootFrameSampler`, report-only, judged from 3.1): the slowest frame from the corpse's open being sent to its close being settled or the sequence abandoned (`HuntScenario.CorpseWindow`); a frame over 50 ms prints `loot_frame_over_50=yes` (a note, never a failure); a review frame saved inside the window is skipped, as the attack sampler skips its own. The leg line now ends `... corpses_opened= corpse_items= looted= loot_frame_max_ms=<x|n/a> loot_skipped= [frame_over_50=yes] [loot_frame_over_50=yes]`.

**The heal actions (M4 3.1 s1).** `IActionApi.UseWithTarget(kit, self)` and `Drink(potion)` return a `HealHandle`; both complete on the UseDone (a kit's and a potion's lines and updates all come before it), so a handle holds the typed `HealResult` (`Healed`, `FailedRoll`, `Drank`, `Wasted`; the amount, the vital, the uses or the stack left, the critical flag) read from the F7E0 line by `HealResult.TryParse` (three shapes: "You [expertly ]heal yourself for N Health points. Your Handy Healing Kit has M uses left.", "You fail to heal yourself. Your ... has M uses left.", "The Potion of Healing restores N points of your Health."; "has 1 use left" and "is used up" are read; nothing allocates but the result). `HealTally.Record(handle)` is the one line the hunt's driver calls: `KitUses`, `Heals`, `FailedRolls`, `Potions`, `Wasted`, `Points`; only a Confirmed handle counts. **A lost roll is a use spent, not an error**, so a kit at low health burns its 20 uses (the live capture: five failed rolls in six at 22% health): the recovery rule heals early or drinks a potion. The trackers are `HealTracker` and `PotionTracker`; the tests are `HealTrackerTests`, `PotionTrackerTests`, `HealLaneTests`, `HealResultTests`, `HealTallyTests` and `HealActionApiTests`.

**The hunt's leg and detail (M4 3.1 s3, s4, p).** The leg line ends `... loot_skipped= heals= kit_uses= potions= ground=belt|forest mode_mismatch=<n>` and then the frame flags (`loot_frame_over_50=yes frame_over_50=yes`). `heals` is the kit uses whose roll won plus the potions that restored something (`heals>=1` is judged from 3.2's gate when `--heal on` and the leg started under `--heal-below`: `HuntLegMeasures.HealthStartShare`, the scenario's first health read, printed as `health_start_pct` on the detail line; the reason is "no heal although the hunt started at N% under the T% trigger"; a leg that started at or above the trigger is not judged on heals); `ground` is the chosen row; `mode_mismatch` is lane g's `CombatModeWatch`, started by `HuntLegRunner.BeginScenario` and read at `Finish` (`HuntLegMeasures.ModeMismatch`), report-only, and nothing judges it. The detail line adds `heal_failed_rolls= heal_points= heal_wasted=` (beside `potions=`, now the confirmed drinks) and `mode_mismatch_first=<the first disagreement or none>`. `looted>=1` is judged over a leg of two or more kills (never on one, and not with `--loot off` or `--hunt-open-corpse off`); `loot_frame_max_ms` over 75 ms fails and over 50 ms ends the line with `loot_frame_over_50=yes` (and `frame_over_50=yes`).

**The live tests (M4 3.1 s4; the lead runs them).** `HuntLiveRun.Run` honours `--hunt-ground` (the options' ground row is passed to the scenario; before s4 the headless hunt always went to the belt) and takes the command-line options (`Run(output, name, quitAfterSeconds, params string[] args)`: `--hunt-kills`, `--hunt-seconds`, `--hunt-ground`, `--heal*`, `--loot*`); the recovery options always travelled inside `HuntOptions`. `HuntLiveTests` gains the loop test (the belt, `--hunt-kills=3 --hunt-seconds=300`, 2400 s limit; prints `hunt_loop kills= looted= loot_skipped= heals= kit_uses= potions= returned=`; asserts `returned=yes`, the verdict composer's `ok` and `kills>=2`). `HealLiveTests` (Loopback; it replaces the capture test, which is removed) has two tests. Test one is Holtburg Hunter in one session through `IActionApi`: a kit at the full vital (the outcome is recorded as `heal_full=`, not asserted), the forest hunt for damage (recovery off, 150 s), at Holtburg a kit use (Confirmed, Healed or FailedRoll, Structure down by one, a Healed amount at most the missing health) and a potion while under the maximum (Confirmed, Drank 1 to 25, the stack down by one), and a second kit use while under 90%; it prints `heal_live kit_uses= healed= failed_rolls= potion_points= wasted= health_before= health_after= heal_full=`. Test two is BSD Mage: a kit from the `item` verb is refused 0x04FC and the kit is dropped, the pack as before; it prints `heal_mage ...`.

**The play-check rows (M4 3.1 s4).** Three rows, in the run's second session, on `PlayChecks.ReplayVisit` (the visit after the reconnect: `HealItems` announces a Handy Healing Kit and five Potions of Healing; the player STARTS with health 5 from the synthetic description, `Recorded = null`, `VitalUpdates` empty; `TODO(m4-standin-start-vitals)`): `heal-potion` (a drink at a partial vital: Confirmed, Drank 1 to 25 and at most the missing health, the vital up by the line's amount, the stack down by one), `heal-kit` (two kit uses queued at once: the first Confirmed as Healed or FailedRoll with the Structure down by one and, for a won roll, the vital up by the amount; the second still pending when the first completed, sent after its UseDone, then Confirmed or refused as a full vital), `heal-potion-at-full-vital` (the vital topped up by kit uses, then a drink: Confirmed, Wasted, the stack down by one, the vital unchanged). No creature, monster or corpse is in any of it (D23): the hunt's heal-between-kills logic is proved by `HuntScenarioTests` and live by the lead. The same rows run without Godot in `HealPlayCheckRowsTests`. The replay visit's stand-in start is logged once as `TODO(m4-standin-start-vitals)` (`StandInVisit.StartVitalsPlaceholder`); a row that needs the recorded character or a full vital belongs in the first session (the seam note for 3.2's vendor and appraisal rows).

## 36. Containers and item moves (M4 2.1; DoD 6, 7)

M4 2.1 gives the client its containers (a side pack's contents, a corpse, a chest) and the item moves on them: pick up,
drop, split and give. The wire is in docs/protocol.md §5.1, §5.3 and §5.4 (GE 0x0196 and 0x0052; GA 0x0195, 0x001B,
0x0055, 0x0056, 0x019B and 0x00CD). This section says where the code is.

**The container model (s1).** `GameStateReducer.Containers.cs` keeps a `ContainerView` per 0196 (its order, each entry's
ContainerType, the foci slot) in `GameState.Containers` (§6). The viewed ground container is the one the player opened: a
second open closes the first (0052 first), and 0052 clears it. The stale objects stay in the object table, as ACE keeps
them. An entry resolves on its F745 after its 0196 (the ground-resolution rule); the 0022-before-F745 and a foreign
container's 0022 (a give to an NPC) are tolerated. `PlayerInventory.SidePacks` is filled from the 0196 at login and on a
pack's arrival. The placement inserts and shifts the other entries (`W/Container.cs` 558-570). `LoginCompleteTracker` waits
for every entry of every side pack that its 0196 names, inside the 3 s cap (§15). The events are `ContainerOpened`,
`ContainerClosed` and `ContainerContentsChanged` (§5). The reducer logs the 0196 once as `TODO(m4-retail-handler-0196)`
(§29's list): no retail handler is cited for it yet.

A 0022 out of the viewed container, and back into it, changes that view's entries in place. The capture's orders (C1 to C4)
are in §16.

**The actions and the trackers (s2).** The kinds, lanes, policy and the busy rule are in §7; the trackers are in §17.
The locked chest's refusal is a `ServerError` with the sound 0x94 as its text (DoD 6).

**The stand-in (s3).** The answers are in §16, in the capture's order; the placeholders are listed in
docs/milestones/m4-plan.md §4.4.

**The hunt's corpse (s4a).** §35 above.

**Play-checks (s4a).** `PlayChecks.Containers.cs` adds three offline rows after the attack rows. Each judges the session's
state (the Action API outcome, `GameState.Containers`, `PlayerInventory` and the object table), not the stand-in's counters:
`container-corpse-opened` (a melee kill of a stand-in tusker leaves a corpse; `OpenContainer` Confirmed; the viewed
container is that corpse and its entries are the Bowl and the Smock), `container-pickup` (`PickUp` of the Bowl Confirmed; the
item is in the main pack and gone from the corpse's view) and `item-drop` (`Drop` Confirmed; no longer held; in the object
table with a position). The rows close the corpse, return to peace and stow the mace. Open:
`TODO(m4-standin-corpse-contents)`. (M4 2.2 s0: the corpse's Bowl and Smock carry a Value (10 and 25), a Burden (15 and 30) and an ItemType the vendor mask covers (Misc 0x80, Clothing 0x4): placeholders, labelled, so the offline hunt loots both under the default rule.)

**The live tests (s4b; the lead runs them).** `ContainerLiveTests` (three tests: a side pack at login on Lifestone
Throwaway, its contents from the 0196; the hunter's own kill's corpse opened and closed, its contents read; Chest 143 opened
and closed, and the locked chest refused with its text) and `ItemMovesLiveTests` (one test on Lifestone Throwaway: a dropped
item picked up; a drop; an Arrow 300 stack split to the pack, to 3D and to wield; a Bag of Rye Seed 30488 given to Alfrin
and accepted; a give refused with 0x046A at Alfrin and at Gawain Cooper). Both are loopback tests on the live backend, all
through `IActionApi`, each printing one verdict line (`containers ...`, `moves ...`). They replace
`ContainerCaptureLiveTests`, which is removed; its research is in cache/m4/2-1/capture.md. The shared helpers are in
`tests/Lifestone.Loopback.Tests/Session/LiveLegs.cs` (`LiveRun.Run` records a headless session to cache/m4/2-1/live;
`LiveLeg` is the step-script base). Run them with `dotnet test tests/Lifestone.Loopback.Tests --filter
"FullyQualifiedName~ContainerLiveTests|FullyQualifiedName~ItemMovesLiveTests" --logger "console;verbosity=detailed"`, about 6
to 9 minutes with the backend up; they are skipped without one.

**Consumers (M4 2.2 s2).** The inventory page (`GameInventoryPage`) reads a side pack's contents through `ContainerViews.TryGet(pack)` and redraws on `ContainerContentsChanged`; the container window (`GameContainerPanel`) reads `ContainerViews.Viewed`, follows `ContainerOpened`, `ContainerClosed` and `ContainerContentsChanged`, and acts only through `IActionApi.CloseContainer` and `IActionApi.PickUp` (`ActionSource.Ui`). Both skip an entry until `ContainerViewEntry.Resolved`. The drops of the minimal drag (`retail-ui.md` section 23.10) go through `MoveToPack`, `PickUp`, `Wield`, and `Drop` in the same way.

**Panel shots of the main pack (M4 3.1 p).** `--play-panel-shots` takes the inventory page twice: `7-inventory-backpack-and-paperdoll.png` with the first side pack selected (unchanged), then `7-inventory-backpack-and-paperdoll-mainpack.png` with the main pack selected (`GameInventoryPage.ShowContents` of the player's own id, as a click on the player's cell does) once `PanelFramesPlan.MainPackListed` holds (the pack list has one cell for each held item whose object landed), or after 60 frames, said by name. The page is left on the main pack, its put-back state. A character with no side pack gets two shots of the same page.

## 37. Vendors (M4 3.2 s1; the close rule and the price model)

**The model.** `GameState.Vendor` / `IGameSessionView.Vendor` is the open vendor's `VendorStock` (null when none): the vendor guid, `Mask` (the ItemType mask it buys; the hunt's sell rule reads it, and `m4-loot-rules-shop-mask` closed on the wire: Renald's 0062 carries 0x480467A7), min and max value, `DealMagicalItems`, `BuyPrice` (what it pays), `SellPrice` (what it charges), the alternate currency, `Entries` (stack -1 unlimited, a listed 0 kept as 0 and not buyable; guid; name, wcid, Value, ItemType, icon) and a `Revision` that rises with every 0062. `PriceToBuy(entry, amount)` = per created stack `max(1, ceil(SellPrice x Value - 0.1))` in float arithmetic; a stackable is chunked by MaxStackSize at the unit Value times the stack size. `PayoutFor(item)` = `max(1, floor(BuyPrice x Value + 0.1))` on the whole stack's Value. `BuysType(itemType)` = `(ItemType & Mask) != 0`. A promissory note (ItemType 0x40000) is charged 1.15 and paid 1.0 (ACE `Vendor.GetSellCost`, `GetBuyCost`, W/Vendor.cs 573-609).

**The trackers follow the capture's order** (`cache/m4/3-2/capture.md`, gitignored). A buy's refreshed 0062 comes before its UseDone; a sale's refreshed 0062 comes before the coins at Thelnoth (C1) and after the UseDone at Renald (C2). The trackers never wait for the refreshed 0062, and the payout's new stack is a new entry (never merged into a held stack).

**The close rule (`TODO(m4-vendor-close-rule)`: the range rule is unread).** ACE's `Vendor.CheckClose` (every 1.5 s) only plays the Close emote when the player's cylinder distance exceeds the UseRadius, and the vendor's close sends no message. Retail's client close is the object-range exit: `gmVendorUI::OnObjectRangeExit` 0x004C0F70 (11.6096), with `gmVendorUI::RecvNotice_CloseVendor` 0x004C0F40, `CM_Vendor::SendNotice_CloseVendor` 0x006AB1D0 and `VendorItemsUI::CloseVendor` 0x004C1210 (the addresses named by the review, 3.2-R4; the range itself is not re-read). The model closes the open vendor when an own position update puts the centres more than UseRadius (0.6 m when the vendor sent none) plus `VendorCloseMarginMetres` (1.0 m, standing for the two cylinder radii) apart, when another vendor's 0062 arrives, when the vendor is deleted (F747) and when the world is left (`LeaveWorld`). A vendor whose object is unknown is never closed by distance. The vendors' UseRadius (3 m for Thelnoth, 5 m for Renald) is read in section 5.9 of `docs/milestones/m4-fights-and-loot.md`.

**Retail's price and open code (not read).** `VendorBuyUI::UpdateTransactionValue` 0x004C3D40, `VendorSellUI::UpdateTransactionValue` 0x004C41A0 and `gmVendorUI::UpdateTotalValue` 0x004C54D0 (11.6096; the review's addresses, 3.2-R4) hold the totals; the prices above are ACE's rule until they are read (`TODO(m4-retail-vendor-price)`). The 0062's consumer is `gmVendorUI::OpenVendor` 0x004C5790, and its sender is `CM_Vendor::SendNotice_OpenVendor` 0x006AB220.

**The shop entry, the loot rule and the sale (M4 3.2 fix).** The hunt enters a shop 1.5 m on the vendor's +X side; the -X side is tried once when that spot is outside the cell or the use radius (`HuntScenario.Vendor.cs`). The loot rule reads Renald's (wcid 718) last 0062 mask this session, else the shard's value, and logs it once as `loot: type mask ... from ...`; an open Thelnoth never changes it. The vendor window's close also closes the model's open vendor (`IGameSessionView.CloseVendorWindow`, which publishes `VendorClosed`), so the window and the model agree. A sale's predicted payout counts only the items that left the pack, and the detail names the items the vendor did not take.

**OpenAC #218 (m4-plan D16), `VendorStockTests.OpenAC_218_*`:** every CreateList row the 0062 carries is listed in the server's order; the packed 0xFFFFFF reads unlimited (-1) and is buyable at any amount; a listed 0 stays 0 and is not buyable.

## 38. The appraisal model (M4 3.2 s2)

`GameState.Appraisals` (`AppraisalCache`, bound 256 guids, the one answered longest ago dropped; counters `Received`, `Appraised`, `Failed`, `NotFound`, `Sequence`) holds each answer. `Appraisal` has the six tables by id, `SpellBook` raw plus `Spells` and `ItemEnchantments` split by the 0x80000000 bit, the profiles, the typed readers and `AppraisalIds`. The reducer is `GameStateReducer.Appraisal.cs`; `IGameSessionView.TryGetAppraisal` reads it. `AppraisalTally` (`Session/Fighter`, fed by `Record(IdentifyHandle)`) counts them: the hunt's `appraised=` is its `Appraised`. The tracker finds its answer by polling the cache (`Sequence` taken at the send), so `ActionTracker` gained no hook.

**Retail.** `ClientUISystem::ExamineObject` is at 0x005657B0 (11.6096; `Opcodes.cs` cites the sender `CM_Item::Event_Appraise` 0x006A94A0). The handler of 00C9 is not read: the candidate is `CM_Item::DispatchUI_AppraiseDone` 0x006A9400 (3.2-R4; `TODO(m4-retail-handler-00c9)`). The unpacker is `AppraisalProfile::UnPack` 0x005B4A30, and the consumer is `gmExaminationUI::RecvNotice_SetAppraiseInfo` 0x004B6E20.

**The examine panel** reads `IGameSessionView.TryGetAppraisal(guid, out Appraisal)` after `IActionApi.Identify(guid, ActionSource.Ui)` completes (`IdentifyHandle.Result`). A Failed result still has the tables (the panel shows them) and no profiles. A failed creature roll was not seen live (capture C3: the hunter's Assess beat Thelnoth's Deception; `TODO(m4-appraise-fail-live)`), so the 5 s cooldown is read from ACE's source only.

## 39. The shortcut bar (M4 3.2 h1)

`SelfState.Shortcuts` is the `ShortcutBar`: 18 slots, `IsMissing(slot)` live from the inventory. The description fills it (`GameStateReducer.Shortcuts.cs`, one call after the inventory) and leaving the world clears it. `ShortcutsChanged` is published per slot changed (section 5). The description's list is `0:0x80001097/0, 1:0x80000AF4/0` after the two adds of capture C4 and empty after their removes (`cache/m4/3-2/capture.md`).

**OpenAC #219.** The reducer's `Leave` (0024, 019A, a 0022 into a foreign container, an F747 of a held object) removes the row and publishes `ItemLeft`. The Action API sends 019D once per update (`ShortcutEvictions`). A move inside the player's packs and a wield keep the row. ACE never removes a row itself (`Player_Character.cs` 245-262). The retail behaviour is the same: `gmToolbarUI::RecvNotice_ServerSaysMoveItem` 0x004BFF40 (11.6096) removes the slot when the item moved and the player no longer owns it (`IsOwnedByPlayer`), with the broadcast on, so #219 is retail's behaviour and not an addition. `UpdateFromPlayerDesc` 0x004C04A0 adds only rows with an object id, and the shortcut number is the slot index.

**The rules still open.** `m4-shortcut-missing-at-login`: a row for an object not held stays and reads missing (retail's `UpdateFromPlayerDesc` adds every row with an object, with no check). `m4-shortcut-eligible-flags`: `IsShortcutEligible` 0x004BDE60 also tests the weenie's stuck bit 0x4 and a virtual answer the decompile does not name; only held and not-pyreal are built. `m4-shortcut-classify`: the client has no WeenieType or BoosterEnum, so a kit and a potion are told by wcid, item type and name until the appraisal model exists. `m4-shortcut-spell`: a spell-only row is kept, and `UseShortcut` answers NotImplemented.

**`UseShortcut`** hands the object to `ItemHolder::UseObject` in retail (or runs the pending target mode first). The kit's self target is a Lifestone choice until `gmToolbarUI::UseShortcut` 0x004BDFE0 is read (review 3.2-R8).
